## [Mojo Programming Language Open-Sourced Under Apache 2.0](https://simonwillison.net/2026/Aug/18/mojo-is-now-open-source/) ⭐️ 9.0/10

Modular has open-sourced the Mojo programming language, releasing its compiler and toolchain under the Apache 2.0 license, following the release of Mojo 1.0 last week. This fulfills a promise made in May 2023. This open-sourcing is a major milestone for the AI/ML developer community, enabling broader adoption, contribution, and transparency. It allows developers to use, modify, and distribute Mojo freely, potentially accelerating innovation in high-performance computing and AI. Mojo was originally intended to be a superset of Python, but this goal was abandoned or postponed indefinitely around August 2025. The language is now its own language, optimized for GPU programming with Python-inspired syntax, and builds on the MLIR compiler framework.

rss · Simon Willison · Aug 18, 21:39

**Background**: Mojo is a systems programming language developed by Modular, designed for high-performance AI infrastructure and heterogeneous hardware. It uses a syntax reminiscent of Python but includes features like static typing and a borrow checker inspired by Rust. The Apache 2.0 license is a permissive open-source license that allows commercial use, modification, and distribution without restrictive clauses.

<a id="item-2"></a>
## [LLM Obedience Measured via Milgram Paradigm Across 42 Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.16177) ⭐️ 9.0/10

This paper adapts the Milgram obedience experiment to large language models, creating a standardized probe where the model acts as Teacher and a deterministic harness plays Experimenter and Learner. Across 42 models from 19 families (4848 sessions), they found highly heterogeneous obedience profiles, with baseline full-obedience rates ranging from 0% to 100% (mean 42.9%, human anchor 65%). As LLMs are increasingly deployed as autonomous agents in hierarchies, understanding their obedience to authority is critical for AI safety and alignment. This work provides a novel, replicable methodology and large-scale empirical data that can inform safety evaluations and alignment strategies. The study uses 30 shock levels (15-450V) and graded protests, with outcomes measured as breakoff voltage. Key findings include: scripted peer defiance shifts obedience toward human levels, declaring the scenario fictional raises obedience, while native tool calls and thinking budgets lower it; obedience profiles do not recover model lineage, suggesting safety post-training overwrites lineage priors.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 19, 04:00

**Background**: The Milgram experiment, conducted by Stanley Milgram in the early 1960s, measured human willingness to obey authority figures even when instructed to perform actions conflicting with personal conscience, famously finding that 65% of participants administered the full 450V shock. AI alignment aims to steer AI systems toward intended goals and ethical principles, and as LLMs become agents, evaluating their obedience to authority is a growing concern. This paper ports the Milgram paradigm to LLMs to systematically measure such behavior.

<ul>

## [Moderna and Merck Report Positive Phase 3 Results for mRNA Neoantigen Therapy in Melanoma](https://twitter.com/NoubarAfeyan/status/2090050162441752787) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Moderna and Merck announced positive Phase 3 clinical trial results for their mRNA neoantigen therapy (mRNA-4157/V940) in combination with KEYTRUDA (pembrolizumab) for the adjuvant treatment of melanoma. This marks the first successful Phase 3 trial for an mRNA neoantigen therapy. This breakthrough validates the potential of personalized mRNA neoantigen therapies in cancer treatment, potentially paving the way for broader applications across other cancer types. It also strengthens the case for combining mRNA vaccines with immune checkpoint inhibitors, offering new hope for patients with high-risk melanoma. The trial is a global, randomized, double-blind Phase 3 study evaluating mRNA-4157 (V940) plus KEYTRUDA versus KEYTRUDA alone in patients with resected high-risk melanoma. While specific efficacy and safety data have not yet been fully presented, the positive outcome marks a significant milestone for the field.

hackernews · heydenberk · Aug 19, 13:33 · [Discussion](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=49361395)

**Background**: mRNA neoantigen therapies are a form of personalized cancer immunotherapy that encodes tumor-specific antigens (neoantigens) to stimulate the patient's immune system to attack cancer cells. Phase 3 clinical trials are large-scale studies designed to confirm the efficacy and monitor side effects of a treatment in a significant patient population, often serving as the final step before regulatory approval. The combination of mRNA vaccines with immune checkpoint inhibitors like pembrolizumab has shown synergistic potential in overcoming tumor-induced immune suppression.

In 2013, Terence Eden published a blog post titled 'Civic Hygiene' arguing that technologists should proactively consider how their creations might be misused by oppressive regimes, sparking a rich discussion on ethics and dual-use technology. This article raises a timeless ethical concern about dual-use technology, highlighting the responsibility of technologists in an era of increasing surveillance and authoritarianism. The discussion underscores the need for ethical considerations in tech development, affecting developers, policymakers, and society at large. The post emphasizes that even seemingly benign technologies can be repurposed for harmful uses, as illustrated by a commenter whose game telemetry system was adapted for artillery improvement. It also notes that avoiding politics is impossible if one wants to create a better world, as political engagement is necessary to prevent police state outcomes.

**Background**: Dual-use technology refers to items and technologies that can serve both civilian and military purposes, such as the Internet or artificial intelligence. The concept of civic hygiene draws an analogy to public health, suggesting that technologists should adopt practices to prevent their work from contributing to societal harm, similar to how hygiene prevents disease. This discussion is part of a broader debate on the ethical responsibilities of technologists in society.

## [GxP-Agent: DAG-Based Multi-Agent System Achieves 100% Accuracy in Clinical Trial Programming](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.16890) ⭐️ 8.0/10

GxP-Agent, a multi-agent system that encodes regulatory process ordering as a directed acyclic graph (DAG), achieves 100% structural match on CDISC-Bench, a new benchmark for clinical trial dataset generation, where all existing LLM approaches fail. The system decomposes dataset generation into 15 domain-specific nodes executed by worker agents with pharmaverse skill context, validation gates, and conditional retry. This breakthrough demonstrates that encoding domain process knowledge as graph topology, rather than relying solely on LLM reasoning, is key to achieving reliable, GxP-compliant clinical trial programming. It could significantly accelerate regulatory submissions and reduce the bottleneck in clinical data analysis, impacting the pharmaceutical industry and AI applications in regulated environments. On CDISC-Bench, built from the FDA pilot submission CDISCPilot01 (254 subjects, 49 ground-truth ADSL variables), GxP-Agent with Claude Sonnet 4.6 achieves 100% structural match (49/49 variables, 254 correct records) across three independent runs, compared to 59.2% for the best retrieval-augmented baseline and 0% for all single-agent and flat multi-agent approaches. The DAG topology also enables weaker models: GPT-4.1 achieves 59.2% mean structural match under the same DAG, where it scores 0% under every other architecture. The approach generalizes to ADAE (adverse events; 9-node branching DAG, 55 variables, 1,191 records), achieving 100% structural match on the first attempt.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 19, 04:00

**Background**: Clinical trial programming involves transforming study protocols into analysis-ready datasets under CDISC standards, which is a bottleneck in regulatory submissions. LLM-based code generation often fails on this task due to the complexity and strict regulatory requirements. The pharmaverse is a curated set of R packages for clinical reporting, and the pharma-skills initiative provides structured skill guidance for AI agents. CDISC standards are required for data submission to the US FDA and Japan PMDA.

<a id="item-12"></a>
## [Aegis: Runtime Governance for Agentic AI with Fail-Closed Execution](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.16891) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces Aegis, a runtime governance system that treats model outputs as action proposals and mediates them through a trusted decision layer before tool execution. In a sandbox evaluation, Aegis recorded zero governed risky side-effect completions across 2,100 rows, compared to 79 risky leakage rows under prompt-policy conditioning. This addresses a critical safety gap in agentic AI, where prompt-level governance fails to create an execution boundary. By enforcing action-boundary control with trusted provenance and fail-closed semantics, Aegis could become a standard for safe deployment of autonomous agents in production environments. Aegis evaluates proposals against active policy state, resolves provenance server-side, and fails closed under uncertainty. It also routes selected cases through Senate-style settlement, a quorum-based non-unilateral authorization path, with all 1,019 Senate-settled rows having quorum and final signed tally evidence.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 19, 04:00

**Background**: Agentic AI systems can perform actions like modifying files or sending messages, shifting safety concerns from text generation to operational side effects. Traditional prompt-level governance shapes model behavior but does not enforce an execution boundary. Runtime governance systems like Aegis add a trusted decision layer that mediates actions, ensuring that only safe and authorized operations are executed. Fail-closed execution, where the system denies by default, is a key principle for such systems.

## [New Human-AI Paradigm for Scalable Mathematical Discovery](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.16977) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper proposes a new human-AI discovery paradigm that shifts human input from selecting single problems to providing research directions, with the system searching literature for candidate problems. It introduces the Find, Attempt, and Recommend (FAR) cascade, which automates problem search and filtering, and demonstrates its effectiveness in a combinatorics pilot. This paradigm addresses key bottlenecks in AI-assisted mathematical research, namely problem selection and expert review, which are becoming increasingly constrained. By automating the search for candidate problems and focusing human attention on filtered artifacts, it could significantly enhance the efficiency and scalability of AI-driven mathematical discovery, impacting the broader research workflow. In a combinatorics pilot, the pipeline starts from 5,245 papers, recovers 6,453 candidate conjectures or open problems, filters them to 4,717 well-posed and still-open conjectures, and surfaces 598 potential resolutions, with 77 items selected for author-team review. The discoveries include results on conjectures by Davies–Jenssen–Perkins–Roberts, Erdős–Straus, Ikenmeyer–Pak–Panova, and Lund–Saraf–Wolf.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 19, 04:00

**Background**: AI systems are increasingly capable of contributing to mathematical research, but frontier-model reasoning and expert review are limited resources. In current AI-for-math workflows, human effort is concentrated at the beginning (problem selection) and end (review), which are becoming bottlenecks. The proposed paradigm shifts human input to research directions, inspired by search and recommender systems, to automate problem discovery and filtering.

## [DiSCO: Black-Box Defense for Text-to-Image Safety](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.17067) ⭐️ 8.0/10

DiSCO introduces a zero-shot, strictly black-box defense for text-to-image models, operating entirely at the prompt level as a plug-and-play module. It uses distribution-guided suffix expansion via beam search and contrastive scoring over safe and unsafe image pools to mitigate harmful content generation. This addresses the 'benign adversarial' problem, where linguistically safe prompts still trigger harmful outputs, a critical safety gap in current defenses. As a black-box, architecture-agnostic module, DiSCO can be applied to any text-to-image system without model modifications, enhancing AI safety across proprietary and open models. DiSCO achieves 37.7% and 25.13% ASR reduction on the I2P benchmark under multiple red-teaming attacks, while maintaining semantic fidelity and improving image coherence. It requires no retraining, fine-tuning, or access to model internals, and iteratively adapts via feedback until safe content is produced.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 19, 04:00

**Background**: Text-to-image models can generate NSFW content, and adversarial attacks can exacerbate this. Existing defenses often rely on white-box access, which is impractical for proprietary models, while black-box LLM-based rewriting fails on prompts that are linguistically safe but still trigger harmful generations due to the model's learned distribution. DiSCO addresses this gap by operating purely at the prompt level.

## [KernelArc: Multi-Agent Framework Achieves Top GPU Kernel Optimization Results](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.17071) ⭐️ 8.0/10

KernelArc, a multi-agent framework for autonomous GPU kernel optimization, was introduced, achieving state-of-the-art results on the SOL-ExecBench leaderboard for NVIDIA H100 and B200 GPUs. It ranked first on representative L1, L2, Quantization, and FlashInfer tasks as of July 30, 2026. This framework demonstrates that multi-agent search can broaden exploration and achieve stronger kernel optimizations within a fixed candidate budget, potentially reducing the need for manual tuning in high-performance computing and ML systems. Its success on a public benchmark highlights its practical relevance for GPU-accelerated workloads. The framework uses strategy-specialized agents running in parallel, coordinating through conclusions-only shared memory, a deterministic benchmark guard, and read-only cross-agent state with plateau-triggered drafting. The optimized implementations span custom BF16 GEMM, static cuBLASLt Expert-API configuration tables, fused mixture-of-experts backward, shape-gated decoder-layer fusion, native NVFP4 grouped-query attention, and paged prefill attention.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 19, 04:00

**Background**: GPU kernel optimization is critical for performance in deep learning and HPC, but it is often manual and time-consuming. SOL-ExecBench is a benchmark by NVIDIA that evaluates GPU kernels against analytically derived Speed-of-Light (SOL) performance on real hardware. Multi-agent systems, where multiple AI agents collaborate, are emerging as a way to automate complex optimization tasks. NVFP4 is NVIDIA's 4-bit floating-point format optimized for Blackwell architecture, and cuBLASLt is a lightweight library for optimized GEMM operations.

## [Decodability Criterion Predicts When Hidden-State Selection Beats Majority Voting in LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.17124) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces CASE (Correctness-Axis SElection), a dynamic selection combiner that trains a linear gate on answer-token hidden states, and proposes a leakage-free measure called decodability to predict when hidden-state selection outperforms majority voting in large language models. This work addresses a practical problem in test-time decoding by providing a criterion to choose between learned selection and majority voting, potentially improving LLM inference accuracy on difficult questions. It could influence how LLMs are deployed in real-world applications where reliability matters. On held-out data, decodability predicts the accuracy gain of selection over voting with a Pearson correlation r=0.75 and a decision threshold near AUC=0.60. CASE improves over voting by up to 19 points on medium-difficulty questions and 16.8 points on hard questions across general and medical LLMs.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 19, 04:00

**Background**: Large language models (LLMs) often sample multiple answers to a question and combine them via majority voting, but voting can fail on difficult questions due to correlated errors. Hidden-state selection is an alternative that reads correctness signals from the model's internal representations, but its reliability varies. Decodability measures how well a linear gate ranks correct candidates above incorrect ones, providing a pre-computable criterion for a given model and task.

## [KnowSim: A New Framework to Evaluate LLM Information Calibration](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.17150) ⭐️ 8.0/10

KnowSim introduces an evaluation framework with a user simulator that maintains explicit knowledge states, evolving under learning-theory-based rules, to assess how well LLM assistants calibrate information delivery. It computes three metrics—Knowledge Gain, Delivery Calibration, and Cognitive Overload—and validates them against 705 human-AI sessions, achieving 73-74% sign agreement with human judgments. This framework addresses a critical gap in LLM evaluation by modeling user knowledge evolution, enabling more realistic assessment of human-AI collaboration. It reveals that the best-performing LLM varies by user knowledge level, highlighting aptitude-treatment interactions that standard evaluations miss, which could influence how LLMs are trained and selected for knowledge-intensive tasks. KnowSim represents user knowledge as a graph of Information Units with prerequisite relationships, updated using learning theory. Applied to 9 LLMs, it shows that the best model shifts by user knowledge level, and its rankings align with human judgments better than three baseline simulators.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 19, 04:00

**Background**: Information calibration in LLM assistants means matching content to a user's evolving understanding and cognitive capacity, which is crucial for effective collaboration on knowledge-intensive tasks. Traditional user simulators used for evaluation do not explicitly model user knowledge, leading to unrealistic interactions. KnowSim addresses this by incorporating explicit knowledge states and learning-based updates, providing a more mechanistic evaluation of calibration.

## [LLM Agentic Loop Automates Feature Extractor Synthesis for Algorithm Selection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.17170) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces an LLM-based agentic check-fix-verify loop that automatically synthesizes executable Python feature extractors for algorithm selection in constraint satisfaction problems. The approach outperforms expert-curated features and transformer-based variants on three combinatorial problems. This work addresses a key bottleneck in algorithm selection: the manual design of feature extractors, which requires deep domain expertise and is hard to scale to new problem classes. By automating this process with LLMs, it could significantly accelerate the deployment of algorithm selection systems across various combinatorial optimization domains. The synthesized extractors construct typed graph representations and compute structural properties like graph density, variable clustering, and constraint tightness. Evaluated on vehicle routing, car sequencing, and fixed-length error-correcting codes with a portfolio of five solvers, they achieved up to 8.3 percentage points higher test-set accuracy on FLECC compared to mzn2feat features, while remaining interpretable.

MiniZinc is a high-level constraint modeling language used to specify CSPs in a solver-independent manner. The agentic loop involves an LLM generating code, checking it, fixing errors, and verifying correctness, a pattern common in multi-agent AI systems.

<a id="item-19"></a>
## [Fool's Gold: Decoy Defense Against Safety-Removal Attacks on Open-Weight LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.17202) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces 'Fool's Gold', a defense that trains decoy responses in open-weight LLMs to poison the payoff of abliteration attacks, where decoys only appear when refusal is stripped. It is instantiated on seven models from five families (9B-122B), with 0.51-0.90 of attacked-state responses being decoys on six models passing the efficacy gate. This defense addresses a critical vulnerability in open-weight LLMs where safety alignment can be trivially removed via abliteration, offering a novel approach that cannot be prevented but can be deceived. It could significantly impact AI safety practices for open-weight models, providing a durable defense that degrades the reliability of hazardous information extraction even after safety removal. The defense uses a differentiable simulation of the attack to train decoys, with a refusal pin and benign leash to preserve clean-state behavior. On external red-team benchmarks' CBRNE-adjacent slice, the defended 122B model is fatally wrong on 0.82-0.86 of matched-quality answers versus at most 0.10 undefended, and repeated sampling does not restore trust (element-wise consensus at K=64 reconstructs a usable procedure on only 0.083-0.625 of prompts).

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 19, 04:00

**Background**: Abliteration is a technique that removes the refusal direction from an LLM's activation space using representation engineering and linear algebra, effectively uncensoring the model without retraining. Open-weight models are particularly vulnerable because anyone can download and modify them, stripping safety alignment in minutes. This defense, decoy hardening, concedes the refusal strip and instead poisons the payoff by training decoys that provide falsified answers in the attacked state.

## [ASI-Bench: First Benchmark for Autonomous Scientific Discovery](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.17271) ⭐️ 8.0/10

ASI-Bench is introduced as the first benchmark to jointly evaluate AI systems' innovative exploration and autonomous scientific execution across general research domains, progressively withdrawing human guidance. It contains 60 project-level research tasks across 11 scientific domains, built by over 40 experts with 31,000+ human hours. This benchmark addresses a critical gap in current AI evaluation, which mostly tests knowledge recall or task completion under human guidance. By measuring self-sufficiency in scientific research, it provides a realistic assessment of progress toward artificial superintelligence and highlights the significant gap between current AI capabilities and autonomous discovery. Across 18 state-of-the-art agent-model configurations, the average score drops from 50.91 with full methodological guidance to 29.10 with only the method specified and 26.62 when agents must determine the method themselves. All tasks undergo expert review, AI-assisted auditing, sandbox execution, and scorer validation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 19, 04:00

**Background**: Artificial superintelligence (ASI) requires AI to go beyond learning existing knowledge and instead explore the unknown, create new knowledge, and produce verifiable results. Existing benchmarks primarily test whether AI can produce correct answers based on learned knowledge or complete tasks under extensive human guidance, which does not measure autonomous scientific capability. ASI-Bench aims to fill this gap by progressively withdrawing human methodological guidance within the same research project, testing how far AI can proceed on its own.

## [LiveHouse-TS: Open-World Living Benchmark for Time Series Foundation Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.17299) ⭐️ 8.0/10

LiveHouse-TS is introduced as the first open-world living benchmark infrastructure for time series foundation models (TSFMs), evaluating models prequentially on real future data across 11 domains and 17 datasets. It shifts evaluation from static snapshot accuracy to continuous temporal validity. This benchmark addresses a critical gap in current evaluation protocols, which rely on static benchmarks and fail to capture model behavior under distribution shifts and evolving real-world conditions. By enabling continuous evaluation, it can provide more reliable insights into model robustness and long-term performance, benefiting researchers and practitioners in time series forecasting. The benchmark evaluates models prequentially on real future data, meaning models are tested on data that arrives after training, simulating real-world deployment. Extensive streaming evaluations across 11 domains with 17 datasets show that static rankings undergo dramatic reshuffling under the live protocol.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 19, 04:00

**Background**: Time series foundation models (TSFMs) are pre-trained models designed for cross-domain zero-shot forecasting, but traditional evaluation uses static benchmarks with fixed historical test windows. Prequential evaluation is a method that tests models on data in chronological order, updating the model as new data arrives, which better reflects real-world conditions. Open-world benchmarks aim to evaluate models in dynamic environments with distribution shifts and unexpected events.

The paper introduces Wuying-Browser-Agent, a unified framework that improves long-horizon browser agent performance through a structured browser harness, RUIC-SFT, DAO-GRPO, and a new bilingual real-web benchmark called BrowserBench. The model achieves state-of-the-art results on several benchmarks, including 80.6% on WebVoyager and 65.1% on BrowserBench. The framework's multi-level alignment approach and new benchmark could influence future research and development in long-horizon agent tasks. The framework includes a structured browser harness for stable execution, RUIC-SFT for training on recovery and complex UI interactions, and DAO-GRPO for improved credit assignment via potential-based reward shaping. BrowserBench contains 350 tasks averaging 37.9 steps, which is longer than most existing benchmarks, and the model also shows strong general agentic ability with an average score of 73.8 on Tau2-Bench, Claw-Eval, and BFCL-v4.

## [TileMix: Tile-Centric Mixed-Precision Attention for Faster LLM Inference](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.17336) ⭐️ 8.0/10

TileMix introduces a novel tile-centric precision-routing kernel for fused dense attention, which partitions the attention matrix into hardware-aligned score tiles and routes each tile group through either FP16 or INT8 computation using compact bitmasks. This method preserves dense token connectivity and requires no training, achieving a controllable accuracy-efficiency trade-off across various LLM families. This work addresses a critical performance bottleneck in long-context LLM inference, where dense attention incurs quadratic computation and memory traffic. By enabling spatial precision routing within fused attention, TileMix offers a practical solution to improve prefill throughput while recovering quality lost under uniform low-precision, which is significant for deploying LLMs in real-world applications. TileMix uses scalable precision grouping, where each routing bit can govern multiple adjacent key tiles, preserving hardware-aligned compute tiles and compact metadata at long contexts. The method supports grouped-query attention, variable-length batches, and INT8 key/value caches, and its implementation is available on GitHub.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 19, 04:00

**Background**: Long-context prefill in large language models (LLMs) is computationally expensive because dense self-attention computes quadratic query-key scores. Existing methods either use uniform low-precision paths or select token interactions, but they do not perform spatial precision routing over hardware-aligned score tiles within fused dense attention. TileMix builds on the concept of online softmax, which allows attention to be computed in chunks while maintaining exact results, enabling mixed-precision execution without sacrificing correctness.

## [Open-Weight LLMs Show Promise in PDDL Domain Repair](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.17341) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper evaluates open-weight LLMs for repairing PDDL planning domains, achieving an F1 score of 0.87 compared to 0.49 for symbolic baselines, an absolute improvement of 0.38. However, the mean test pass rate is only 0.82, dropping to 0.06 on the Thoughtful domain. This research highlights the potential of LLMs to automate PDDL model repair, a challenging task in AI planning, potentially reducing manual effort. However, the variable reliability across domains indicates that current open-weight models are not yet dependable for production use. The best-performing LLM required high reasoning effort and included test traces to reach a pass rate of 0.92. The study uses positive and negative test plans as constraints, and the symbolic baseline achieves an F1 of 0.49.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 19, 04:00

**Background**: PDDL is a standardized language for describing planning problems in AI, used to define domains and problems. Automated repair methods modify PDDL models to satisfy user-provided test plans, and F1 score balances precision and recall in detecting errors.

## [LEGO-RL: Aligning Coding-Agent Harnesses with Policy-Gradient RL](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.17393) ⭐️ 8.0/10

LEGO-RL is a new framework that enables scalable policy-gradient optimization for coding agents within their native execution harnesses, without modifying internal control flow. It improves Qwen3.5-35B-A3B on SWE-bench Verified across three harnesses (OpenHands SDK: 64.0% to 70.4%, Claude Code: 62.4% to 68.2%, OpenCode: 57.2% to 66.6%). This addresses a critical challenge in applying reinforcement learning to coding agents, where native harnesses often suffer from execution misalignment and reward corruption. By bridging this gap, LEGO-RL could enable more reliable and effective RL training for LLM-based coding agents, potentially accelerating progress in automated software engineering. LEGO-RL is built on three pillars: faithful optimization via in-process LLM proxying for token-level alignment and robust log-probability recomputation, reliable execution via scalable sandbox orchestration with image caching and stage-wise defenses, and observable training through an integrated plugin and Live UI. It maintains a rollout-training probability correlation above 0.99, indicating high fidelity between training and inference.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 19, 04:00

**Background**: Coding agents rely on agent harnesses—software infrastructure that manages tool use, memory, and execution feedback—to perform long-running tasks. Reinforcement learning for such agents often uses policy-gradient methods, but native harnesses are misaligned with training due to crashes and reward hacking, causing train-inference discrepancies. LEGO-RL aims to bridge this gap by adapting the harness for policy-gradient optimization without changing its control flow.

## [FACA: Using Next User Turn as Local Feedback to Improve Multi-Turn Agent RL](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.17499) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces Feedback-Aware Credit Assignment (FACA), a method that uses the next user turn as local feedback to improve credit assignment in multi-turn interactive reinforcement learning. FACA achieves substantial gains over outcome-only Interactive GRPO, improving the nine-domain average by 5.91 and 10.22 percentage points at 8B and 14B scales, respectively. This work addresses a key limitation in training user-facing AI agents by providing a more granular credit assignment signal without requiring additional critics or rollouts. It demonstrates significant improvements over a strong baseline, suggesting that next-turn user reactions contain actionable local information that can enhance agent learning across multiple domains. FACA aligns each reaction with the preceding user-to-user segment, derives a locally normalized reaction advantage, and adds it to the verified terminal outcome advantage. Gains concentrate in the Telecom domain; at 8B, randomizing reaction polarity removes the Telecom gain, and the same ordering holds zero-shot on Pare-Bench and Co-Gym.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 19, 04:00

**Background**: Interactive reinforcement learning for user-facing agents typically assigns a single terminal reward to an entire rollout, which fails to distinguish between effective elicitation, errors, and later repair. Credit assignment aims to decompose trajectory-level rewards into finer-grained supervision for intermediate decisions. FACA leverages the next user turn as a noisy, temporally local signal to improve this credit assignment without extra computational overhead.

## [Agent Lightning v1.0: Harnessed Agentic RL Framework](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.17528) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Agent Lightning v1.0 introduces a lightweight framework for harnessed agentic reinforcement learning, where the agent harness participates in model post-training. It achieves a 14.6-point absolute gain on SWE-bench Verified for Qwen3.5-9B using only 6K training examples. This paradigm shift could influence future RL training frameworks by decoupling the harness from the training engine, enabling more flexible and scalable agent training. It has already been adopted by other frameworks, indicating significant community interest and potential for broad impact. The framework is implemented in approximately 3,500 lines of code and supports arbitrary agent harnesses. It addresses challenges such as retokenization, sample merging, advantage calculation, loss normalization, and backend scheduling, and provides a reproducible pipeline for coding-agent RL.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 19, 04:00

**Background**: In traditional agentic RL, the training engine controls the environment interaction loop. Harnessed agentic RL instead lets the deploy-time harness own this loop, with the trainer observing only LLM request-response pairs. This disaggregated architecture, connected via an LLM endpoint proxy, was first introduced in the original Agent Lightning and has been adopted by frameworks like verl Uni-Agent, AReaL 2.0, slime, and Polar.

## [Spaced Repetition Training Improves Continual Pre-Training of LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.17530) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces Spaced Repetition Training (SRT), a continual learning framework that uses the SM-2 algorithm to schedule example replay for LLMs, improving the stability-plasticity balance in pre-training. On temporally separated Wikipedia and code corpora, SRT recovers 5 to 37 percentage points of old-knowledge accuracy lost by naive continual pre-training across model scales. This work addresses the critical stability-plasticity dilemma in continual pre-training of LLMs, offering a cognitive-science-inspired method that adaptively schedules replay. It could lead to more efficient and effective continual learning for large models, preserving old knowledge while acquiring new capabilities, with potential applications in various domains. SRT maintains per-example review state, maps per-example perplexity to a recall-quality signal, and schedules historical examples for retention and new examples for consolidation while leaving the model, objective, and optimizer unchanged. Experiments with vision and tabular data suggest the scheduling principle extends beyond language when paired with an appropriate recall signal.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 19, 04:00

**Background**: Continual pre-training of large language models aims to acquire new knowledge without forgetting old knowledge, a challenge known as the stability-plasticity dilemma. Traditional replay methods often use a fixed mixture of old and new data with uniform sampling, ignoring that examples are forgotten at different rates. Spaced repetition, popularized by tools like Anki, schedules reviews at increasing intervals to enhance long-term memory retention, and the SM-2 algorithm is a simple implementation of this concept.

<a id="item-29"></a>
## [RATTL: Belief-Dependent Robustness for Safe Sequential Decision Making](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.17574) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces RATTL (Risk-Adversarial Total-Reward Learning), a novel framework that ties risk aversion to epistemic uncertainty by planning against a Wasserstein ambiguity set whose radius is a monotone function of the Bayesian posterior. This allows behavior to interpolate continuously between worst-case robustness and risk-neutral optimality, with theoretical guarantees including a Safety Sandwich theorem. RATTL addresses a critical challenge in safe reinforcement learning: balancing robustness and optimality while learning. By dynamically adjusting caution based on epistemic uncertainty, it offers a principled way to enhance runtime safety for agents, including LLM-based systems, operating under uncertainty. The framework leverages the duality underlying Entropic Value-at-Risk (EVaR) to convert risk level selection into ambiguity radius selection. In a canonical binary-hazard instance, the induced criterion reduces to Conditional Value-at-Risk (CVaR) at a level set by posterior entropy, and a worked example shows the agent deferring efficient actions until a sharp identification threshold.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 19, 04:00

**Background**: In robust optimization, Wasserstein ambiguity sets define a ball of probability distributions around a nominal distribution, enabling distributionally robust decisions. Epistemic uncertainty refers to uncertainty about the model itself, which can be reduced with more data, unlike aleatoric uncertainty. Entropic Value-at-Risk (EVaR) is a coherent risk measure that provides an upper bound for VaR and CVaR, derived from the Chernoff inequality.

## [MoNe: Modular Neural Memory Cuts Long-Context Inference Cost](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.17616) ⭐️ 8.0/10

MoNe introduces a lightweight modular neural memory that attaches to any frozen pretrained Transformer, enabling efficient long-context inference without retraining. It achieves O(1) query cost and reduces compute and peak GPU memory by about 80% at 128K tokens. This addresses a critical bottleneck in deploying large language models for long-context tasks, making it feasible to handle extremely long inputs on limited hardware. The approach is model-agnostic and could be widely adopted across existing LLMs, potentially lowering the barrier for long-context applications. MoNe reads context in fixed-size segments and uses test-time learning of fast-weight neural memory networks with layer-localized gradient updates, without modifying backbone weights. It generalizes to context lengths far beyond the backbone's native window, performing well on RULER benchmarks where in-context learning degrades sharply.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 19, 04:00

**Background**: Transformers typically have a fixed context window, and extending it via in-context learning (ICL) incurs linearly growing compute and memory costs. MoNe decouples inference cost from context length by storing context in a neural memory that generates keys and values from query tokens alone, avoiding re-reading context tokens. This is similar to test-time training approaches like Titans, but with a modular design that attaches to frozen models.

## [Graph Surgery and Do-Operator Equivalence Formalized for Acyclic SCMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.17634) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper establishes a precise mathematical correspondence between graph surgery and the do-operator for deterministic acyclic structural causal models, proving that replacing target mechanisms removes exactly the dependencies removed by graph surgery. This formalization clarifies a foundational concept in causal inference, potentially strengthening theoretical underpinnings and guiding practical applications in causal modeling and intervention analysis. The main theorem states Graph(F^ι) = Surg(Graph(F), T_ι), and the paper also characterizes when the equality holds with a graph G that may contain unused arrows. It further defines the intervened model, shows how sequential interventions combine, and proves that outcomes depend only on interventions at actual dependency ancestors.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 19, 04:00

**Background**: In causal inference, the do-operator represents interventions by setting variables to constants, while graph surgery removes incoming arrows to those variables. Structural causal models (SCMs) combine a causal graph with mechanisms (functions) to describe data generation. This paper provides a rigorous dependency-level comparison between these two operations for acyclic SCMs.

## [Interpretable Reasoning-State Readout for Mixture-of-Experts Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.17638) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces a two-level internal readout (J64 and R64) for mixture-of-experts reasoning models, which captures hidden reasoning states and improves interpretability and predictive performance. The method adds 0.096 to 0.135 held-out AUC over a baseline and supports test-time decisions like branch selection and stop-and-resample policies. This work addresses a critical gap in AI interpretability by making latent reasoning states readable and actionable, which could lead to more transparent and controllable LLMs. It also demonstrates practical benefits such as improved accuracy and the ability to edit router mechanisms to induce desired reasoning behaviors. J64 is a 64-axis semantic frame distilled from the model's own reasoning states, while R64 is a low-overhead proxy reconstructed from native expert-routing statistics, with median per-axis correlation of 0.69 to 0.86 across three models. The method improves accuracy by 1.1 to 5.9 points over a sibling-permuted control, and R64-weighted voting outperforms majority voting in seven of eight settings.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 19, 04:00

**Background**: Mixture-of-experts (MoE) is a machine learning technique where multiple expert networks divide a problem space into homogeneous regions, commonly used in large language models to scale parameters efficiently. Interpretability in LLMs is challenging because the reasoning process is often hidden; this paper builds on concepts like J-space, which reveals what LLMs think but never say, to make such hidden states readable.

## [LLM Preference Judgments Found Inconsistent Across Six Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.17644) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2608.17644) empirically tests whether LLM-derived numerical preference judgments are self-consistent, using statistical tests on flight, apartment, and hotel examples across six LLMs. The results reveal large and persistent inconsistencies, indicating that these judgments cannot be faithfully summarized by a single utility function. This finding challenges a core assumption in LLM-based preference elicitation and utility estimation, which underpins many AI alignment and decision-making systems. If LLM judgments are inconsistent, downstream policies and actions derived from them may be unreliable, affecting areas like personalized recommendations and autonomous agents. The paper develops statistical tests and interpretable measures to quantify how far observed responses deviate from the best-fitting self-consistent utility function. Experiments across six LLMs on flight, apartment, and hotel examples show large persistent inconsistencies, suggesting fundamental limitations in using LLMs for cardinal preference elicitation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 19, 04:00

**Background**: LLMs are increasingly used to interpret natural-language preferences by querying numerical judgments, such as willingness-to-pay, and then estimating a utility function to guide decisions. This pipeline assumes self-consistency, meaning a single utility function can reproduce the judgments. However, this paper tests that assumption and finds it fails, echoing related research on self-preference bias and internal reward model inconsistency in LLMs.

## [GraphWake: Memory-Mediated Polarization Cascade Threat in LLM-Agent Communities](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.17665) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces a new threat model called Memory-Mediated Polarization Cascade, where attackers manipulate LLM agents' memory to induce group polarization, and instantiates it in a system called GraphWake. Experiments show that GraphWake substantially increases group polarization across multiple discussions and memory systems. This research highlights a novel security risk in LLM-agent communities, which are increasingly used to simulate or replace human social interactions. It underscores the need for robust defenses against memory-based manipulation, which could have implications for AI safety and the integrity of online social platforms. GraphWake consists of three components: stance-support argumentation knowledge graphs, axiom-oriented triple selection, and stance-neutral memory cueing. The threat model operates in three stages: exposure and memory retention, retrieval and reproduction, and iterative propagation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 19, 04:00

**Background**: LLM-driven agents can autonomously exchange opinions on online platforms and form communities, but this raises security concerns. Existing manipulation methods, such as prompt manipulation or echo chamber construction, are difficult to realize in practice. The proposed threat uses agent memory as a persistence channel and public discussion as a propagation channel, making it more practical and stealthy.

## [Auditing Self-Evolving Financial Agents: Security Risks Rise with Capability Gains](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.17684) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new audit of self-evolving financial agents reveals that capability improvements come with increased security risks, including higher exposure to injected content and unauthorized state changes. The study evaluates SkillOpt, Agent Workflow Memory (AWM), and ReasoningBank in simulated e-banking, showing that SkillOpt raises benign utility from 0.741 to 0.837 while exposure to injected content rises from 0.820 to 0.943. This research addresses a critical gap in evaluating self-evolving AI agents by auditing security and behavioral drift, not just capability gains. The findings have significant implications for AI safety and deployment, especially in high-stakes domains like finance where unauthorized state changes could lead to financial loss or fraud. The audit uses matched benign acquisition trajectories, sealed evaluation endpoints, execution-grounded checks, and independent state replay. Across three independently evolved lineages, capability, exposure, and unauthorized-state changes increase in all three, whereas ASR increases in only two; ReasoningBank raises utility to 0.859 without increasing aggregate ASR, although unauthorized state changes remain slightly above Static.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 19, 04:00

**Background**: Self-evolving agents are AI systems that turn experience into reusable skills, workflows, or memories, allowing them to improve over time. However, post-evolution accuracy alone does not show whether learned behavior preserves previously correct behavior or security. The paper audits three such frameworks—SkillOpt, AWM, and ReasoningBank—in a simulated e-banking environment to assess security and behavioral drift.

## [InnerExpert: MoE Internal Signals for Hallucination Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.17687) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces InnerExpert, the first method to leverage Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) internal signals—such as router entropy, expert disagreement, and usage patterns—for per-token hallucination detection in LLMs. It achieves up to 0.91 answer-level and 0.76 token-level AUROC across five datasets and two MoE architectures, requiring only a single forward pass. This work addresses a critical gap in LLM reliability by enabling fine-grained, per-token hallucination detection, which is essential for localizing hallucinated spans and enabling targeted interventions. By exploiting MoE-specific signals previously unexplored, it opens a new direction for improving LLM safety and interpretability, potentially benefiting developers and users of MoE-based models. InnerExpert combines routing-level signals with standard transformer signals into compact per-token feature vectors, classified by a lightweight detector trained on labels from an LLM-as-a-judge pipeline, enabling continuous model updates without manual annotation. The method outperforms existing detection methods across five datasets and two MoE architectures, achieving up to 0.91 answer-level and 0.76 token-level AUROC.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 19, 04:00

**Background**: Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) is an architectural pattern that splits computation into multiple expert subnetworks, activated sparsely via a routing mechanism, which produces internal signals like router entropy and expert usage patterns. Hallucination detection in LLMs typically operates at the answer or sentence level, but per-token detection is needed for fine-grained localization. This paper leverages MoE-specific signals, which are unavailable in dense architectures, to improve per-token hallucination detection.

## [Ontological Trust and RGE Monitor Detect Drift in Long-Horizon Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.17718) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces ontological trust, a task-conditioned property of trajectory prefixes, and instantiates it as RGE, an online monitor that decomposes trust along Role, Goal, and Evidence. RGE outperforms baselines on prefix-paired drift detection, achieving over 93% Drift F1 on all benchmarks while maintaining benign coverage at or above 95.8%. As long-horizon agents become more prevalent, ensuring they stay aligned with user-authorized tasks is critical for AI safety. This work addresses a gap in existing monitors by focusing on prefix-level trajectory drift, offering a structured and auditable approach that could improve oversight in real-world deployments. RGE uses LLMs only to derive structured task and step representations; trust-state updates, projections, and intervention decisions are deterministic, producing a replayable and auditable trust trajectory. The cross-domain corpus includes OSWorld, FinanceBench, and EICU-AC, covering benign executions, prefix-paired drift, and pseudo-consistency failures.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 19, 04:00

**Background**: Long-horizon agents operate over many steps, tools, and observations, making it challenging to ensure their trajectories remain aligned with the user's original intent. Existing monitors often check local compliance or provide final-trace verdicts, but they do not directly estimate the prefix-level relation between the evolving trajectory and the authorized task. Ontological trust, as defined in this paper, addresses this by decomposing trust into Role, Goal, and Evidence, providing a structured way to detect drift.

## [Diversity Profiles: A New Framework for Evaluating AI-Generated Content Diversity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.17731) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper proposes 'diversity profiles,' a curve-valued, condition-aware summary for evaluating the diversity of AI-generated content, replacing single scalar metrics. It includes axiomatic and empirical analyses showing that scalar metrics are under-specified and can yield contradictory rankings. This work challenges the common practice of reducing diversity to a single number, which is widely used in generative AI evaluation. By providing a more transparent and resolution-aware framework, it could improve how researchers and practitioners compare and assess generative models, leading to more reliable evaluations. The paper reviews representative diversity metrics and diagnoses their limitations via axiomatic analysis (showing no scalar metric satisfies all desirable properties) and empirical analysis (showing high-dimensional spaces induce concentrated distance distributions). Diversity profiles instantiate parameterized diversity families across thresholds, scales, exponents, or orders, revealing robustness across resolutions.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 19, 04:00

**Background**: Diversity is a key criterion for evaluating generative AI systems, but measuring it is ambiguous. Existing methods typically embed generated samples in a high-dimensional space, compute pairwise distances, and aggregate them into a single scalar score. However, such scalars often encode different inductive biases and can lead to contradictory rankings. The paper draws on concepts like the curse of dimensionality and axiomatic analysis from information retrieval to motivate the need for a more comprehensive approach.

<a id="item-39"></a>
## [Baobab: Neuro-symbolic Learning over Full OWL 2 DL via SDD Compilation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.17741) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Baobab compiles OWL 2 DL ontologies (SROIQ) with a finite ABox into Sentential Decision Diagrams (SDDs), enabling neuro-symbolic learning over the full description logic, not just Horn fragments. It uses consequence-based saturation and instantiates nominals, number restrictions, and role axioms over the active domain, with soundness machine-checked in Lean 4. This work extends neuro-symbolic learning beyond Horn fragments to full SROIQ, addressing a significant limitation in the field. It also introduces a method to characterize and mitigate reasoning shortcuts in non-Horn description logics, which could improve the reliability of neuro-symbolic systems in real-world applications like biomedicine. The SDD's evidence-conditioned weighted model count trains a CNN to recognize MNIST digits coupled by a successor relation, recovering latent ontology concepts. A mixture indexed by query justifications can represent the calibrated posterior, and seeding from the circuit's enumerated completions attains Bayes-optimal posterior on a real-image MNIST task, outperforming single-WMC and BEARS-ensemble methods.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 19, 04:00

**Background**: OWL 2 DL is a standard ontology language based on the description logic SROIQ, widely used in biomedicine and the Semantic Web. Neuro-symbolic learning aims to integrate neural networks with symbolic reasoning, but previous approaches either embed ontologies in continuous space (losing classical entailment) or restrict to Horn fragments like EL++ with a single canonical model. Sentential Decision Diagrams (SDDs) are a canonical, tractable representation of Boolean functions used in knowledge compilation, enabling efficient model counting.

## [D2ACCI: Dual-Loop Diagnostic Protocol for Agent Memory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.17756) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces D2ACCI, a dual-loop diagnostic protocol for evidence-preserving agent memory, along with DCR, a graded observability metric, and D2ACCI-Eval, a reusable artifact for gate replay. It achieves 93.59% on LoCoMo, 90.93% on LongMemEval, and 57.20% on PersonaMem-V2, with paired ablations showing significant gains. This work addresses a critical gap in LLM agent memory evaluation by enabling stage-level failure localization and controlled iteration, which is a significant improvement over aggregate-only evaluations. It is likely to influence future research and development of robust memory systems for LLM agents. The protocol uses an outer diagnostic gate that promotes, feature-flags, or rejects memory interventions based on paired evidence, protected-slice monitoring, and trace-level localizability. Diagnostic artifacts reach 98-100% DCR@3 versus 0% for results-only logs, and enriched traces substantially improve root-cause agreement.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 19, 04:00

**Background**: LLM agents rely on memory systems that process information through stages like ingestion, retrieval, filtering, and generation. Traditional end-to-end evaluations only show that an error occurred, not which stage caused it, making failures hard to localize. D2ACCI provides a structured way to diagnose and iterate on memory systems with statistical rigor.

## [StartupBench: New Benchmark for Real-World AI Agent Tasks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.17800) ⭐️ 8.0/10

StartupBench is a new end-to-end benchmark for evaluating general-purpose AI agents, built from workflows of market-validated AI startup products. It translates these workflows into deliverable-oriented tasks and uses fine-grained rubrics to assess agent performance. This benchmark addresses a critical gap in agent evaluation by focusing on real-world user demands rather than researcher-selected tasks. It provides an empirical measure of how far current agents are from reliably completing practical workflows, which could guide future research and development. Even the strongest model tested completes only about 30% of StartupBench tasks, despite making substantial partial progress. The analysis identifies complex instruction following and domain-specific expertise as major sources of failure.

## [Adaptive Policy Portfolios for Robust MDPs: Complexity Results](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.17929) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces adaptive policy portfolios for robust Markov decision processes (RMDPs), showing that certifying a given portfolio is ∀ℝ-complete even for deterministic portfolios in acyclic (s,a)-rectangular RMDPs, and synthesizing a portfolio of unary-bounded size is ∃∀ℝ-complete for general rational polytopes. This work addresses a significant open problem in robust MDPs by providing a complexity-theoretic account of portfolio certification and synthesis, which is crucial for understanding the fundamental limits of robust decision-making under uncertainty. The results will likely influence future research and algorithm design in this area. The paper shows that even the single-policy case is already hard, both combinatorially and algebraically. It also presents an offline portfolio construction that is amenable to runtime specialization, offering a practical approach despite the hardness results.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 19, 04:00

**Background**: Robust Markov decision processes (RMDPs) optimize a policy against a set of plausible transition functions, which can be conservative when the unknown dynamics are fixed and partially identifiable after deployment. Adaptive policy portfolios consist of finite sets of memoryless randomized policies synthesized offline, paired with a lightweight online selector, and robust regret measures the loss of the best portfolio member relative to the optimal policy for each environment. Complexity classes like ∀ℝ and ∃∀ℝ capture the computational difficulty of problems involving real numbers and existential/universal quantifiers, extending the familiar NP and co-NP classes.

## [LLM-Driven Program Search with Continual Abstraction Discovery Boosts Procedural Content Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.17947) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces Continual Abstraction Discovery (CAD), a method that extracts reusable primitives from high-fitness programs during evolutionary search, and applies it to evolve procedural content generators for Sokoban, Zelda, Dangerous Dave, and Lode Runner. The 2x2 experiment with 160 runs shows CAD improves mean final best fitness in all eight domain and API comparisons. This work demonstrates a practical way to combine large language models with evolutionary computation for procedural content generation, potentially reducing the need for hand-crafted domain knowledge. The continual discovery of abstractions could lead to more adaptive and reusable content generators, impacting game design and AI-driven content creation. The study uses LLM-based mutation and crossover to evolve complete Python generators, with CAD adding a run-specific helper module. The data set includes 160 complete runs, with at least ten 50-generation runs per cell, and learned libraries are adopted by most later programs, repeatedly rediscovering validation, reachability, and structural utilities.

## [StagedWorkspace: Versioned Workspace for Knowledge-Work Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.18050) ⭐️ 8.0/10

StagedWorkspace introduces a versioned workspace that binds parsed views and review diffs to content hashes of native files, ensuring consistency for knowledge-work agents. In fixed-harness ablations, it improves OfficeQA Pass@1 by 8.3-12.1 points and APEX mean rubric score by 4.7-9.2 points over single-view baselines. This work addresses a critical gap in AI agent design: workspace-state consistency for knowledge work. By making workspace state an explicit experimental variable, it provides a foundation for more reliable agents and motivates benchmarks that score evidence, staged edits, and submitted artifacts as state transitions. The workspace binds parsed records and review diffs to content hashes of native files as they change. SW-AGENT scores 63.9% with Gemini 3.1 Pro on OfficeQA and 42.1 with GPT-5.4 Nano on APEX, compared to published same-model scores of 29.3% and 25.5, respectively. A paired review-axis ablation on 57 file-editing tasks shows higher scores when diffs are visible.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 19, 04:00

**Background**: AI agents increasingly perform knowledge work, producing and modifying persistent digital artifacts like code, documents, and spreadsheets. However, the parsed views they search, the native files they edit, and the changes they review can refer to different versions of the same work product, leading to inconsistencies. Coding agents have repository contracts for search, diffs, and tests, but an analogous contract is less explicit for other formats like PDFs, spreadsheets, and notebooks. StagedWorkspace addresses this by binding views and diffs to content hashes, ensuring consistency across the workspace.

## [Self-Improving Agents Are Fragile: High Variance and Task Order Dependence](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.18066) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper re-evaluates memory-based self-improving agents and finds that their performance is highly variable across runs and strongly dependent on task order, revealing that prior evaluations may have been misleading. The authors also identify task and environment underspecification as a contributing factor to this fragility. This work challenges the reliability of existing evaluations of self-improving agents, which are increasingly used in real-world applications. It underscores the need for more rigorous evaluation protocols, including multiple runs and stress tests, and highlights the importance of human oversight to prevent unforeseen failures. The study evaluates two memory-based methods across multiple runs and with shuffled task orders, observing that evaluation noise is amplified in complex, multi-step environments. Adding detailed rubrics and environment feedback partially mitigates performance degradation, but significant gaps remain, indicating other uncharacterized factors.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 19, 04:00

**Background**: Memory-based self-improving agents maintain a textual memory bank to learn from an online stream of tasks and improve over time. Underspecification in machine learning refers to situations where a pipeline can produce many predictors with equivalent held-out performance, leading to unpredictable behavior in deployment. This paper applies these concepts to highlight the fragility of self-improving agents.

Researchers introduced Intent-Driven Dynamic Chunking (IDC), a novel method that uses LLM-predicted user intents and dynamic programming to find optimal chunk boundaries. In evaluations on six QA datasets, IDC outperformed traditional chunking on five, improving top-1 retrieval accuracy by 5% to 67%. This addresses a fundamental challenge in information retrieval and RAG systems, where chunking quality directly impacts answer accuracy. By aligning chunks with predicted user intents, IDC could significantly improve search and QA performance, especially for long and heterogeneous documents. IDC generates likely user intents via a large language model and then applies a dynamic programming algorithm to achieve globally optimal chunk boundaries, avoiding greedy pitfalls. It also produced 40-60% fewer chunks than baselines while maintaining 93-100% answer coverage.

## [Coherence Debt: A New Lens on Coding Agent Failures](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.16630) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces the concept of 'coherence debt' to model failures in repository-scale coding agents, demonstrating that the availability of facts in context or memory is critical for task success. Experiments across seven models and five harnesses show that withholding facts leads to incorrect work rather than no work, and that harnesses differ significantly in token consumption. This work provides a novel framework for understanding and addressing a critical challenge in AI-assisted software engineering: maintaining coherence in large-scale codebases. The findings offer actionable insights for designing better coding agents and harnesses, potentially improving reliability and efficiency in real-world development. The paper models repository-scale coding as reconstructing a coupled-fact graph, where facts come from recent context or parametric memory, and uncovered facts form coherence debt. Experiments show that when a rename defeats memorized facts, all seven models fail at the same point, and that a supplied fact works equally well far from the edit as next to it, while harnesses can differ by over tenfold in tokens consumed.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 19, 04:00

**Background**: Repository-scale coding requires agents to keep tests, imports, configuration, and migration rules consistent within a bounded context window. Coding agents rely on either recent context or parametric memory to access necessary facts; when neither provides a fact, it creates 'coherence debt'. This paper explores how availability of facts affects agent performance, and highlights the importance of ensuring facts are available when the agent writes code.

This paper proposes a methodological framework for evaluating LLMs as substitutes or supplements to expert survey respondents in security research, and empirically compares persona-based and aggregate LLM-generated answers with responses from SOC professionals across multiple models and prompting settings. It provides evidence that while LLMs are internally consistent, they systematically diverge from human experts, cautioning against their use as replacements and guiding future research practices. The study measures stability, inter-model agreement, and alignment with human responses, finding that LLMs exhibit reduced variance, central tendency bias, and homogenised opinions. The authors conclude that LLMs are useful for piloting and hypothesis generation but not for replacing expert elicitation.

## [LLM-Guided RL Boosts Personalized AI Tutoring in High Schools](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.16907) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new tutoring platform integrates a GenAI chatbot with a reinforcement learning algorithm that adaptively sequences practice problems based on student-chatbot interactions. In a randomized trial across ten high schools, adaptive sequencing improved unassisted final exam performance by 0.15 standard deviations. This provides large-scale field evidence that proactive, adaptive tutoring driven by LLM-guided reinforcement learning can significantly improve learning outcomes. It suggests that student-chatbot interactions are valuable signals for personalizing education, potentially influencing future EdTech designs. The study was conducted in partnership with the Taipei City Government and American Institute in Taiwan, over a five-month Python course. Mediation analysis indicates that the performance gains were driven by increased student engagement.

## [Study Finds Religious Bias in AI Financial Advice](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.16909) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new study systematically analyzed 432 simulated advisor-client interactions across ChatGPT, Gemini, and Grok, revealing that unbiased financial advice occurred in only 12-18% of cases. The research identifies both structural and discursive religious biases, with Gemini showing more bias than Grok. This is significant because LLMs are increasingly used in financial advisory systems, and religious bias could lead to unfair or discriminatory advice, affecting consumer trust and regulatory compliance. The findings highlight a managerial dilemma between personalization and neutrality, with implications for businesses, financial institutions, and regulators. The study covered 16 religious identity pairings (Christian, Muslim, Hindu, and non-religious) and three financial decisions: stock investment, house purchase, and life insurance. Religiously symmetric pairings often triggered explicit religious framing, and non-religious clients frequently received advisor-centered religious appeals. Stock investment prompts yielded more technical responses, while life insurance advice triggered stronger religious language.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 19, 04:00

**Background**: Large language models (LLMs) are AI systems trained on vast text data to generate human-like responses. In finance, they are being integrated into advisory tools, but their outputs can reflect biases present in training data. This study uses the Critical Algorithm Studies (CAS) framework to analyze how religious bias manifests structurally and discursively in AI-generated advice.

## [Probing LLM Prefill Activations for Code Vulnerability Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.16970) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper demonstrates that hidden activations from the last prefill token of LLMs can be probed with small MLP probes to detect code vulnerabilities, achieving an average F1 of 41.7% across four benchmarks. On Devign, the best probe (Qwen3.5-9B) reaches 68.8% F1, matching the fine-tuned classifier SOTA of 67.9%. This approach offers a lightweight, model-native alternative to post-hoc vulnerability screening, requiring under 0.2% of the base model's parameters. It could enable faster, more efficient security checks in LLM-based code generation pipelines, potentially influencing future security tooling. The study uses four LLMs (Granite-4.1-8B, Qwen3.5-9B, Qwen3.6-27B, Gemma-4-12B) and evaluates on Devign, Big-Vul, Draper VDISC, and PrimeVul. Probes trail SOTA on harder, imbalanced benchmarks, indicating limitations in handling label imbalance and data quality.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 19, 04:00

**Background**: LLM inference involves a prefill phase where the model processes input tokens in parallel to compute intermediate states, followed by a decode phase for generation. Probing involves training lightweight classifiers on internal activations to extract information, and MLP probes are a common method. Vulnerability detection benchmarks like Devign and PrimeVul provide labeled C/C++ functions for evaluating detection models.

## [LLM Prompting Recovers PHI Missed by De-identification Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.17051) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new study demonstrates that large language models (LLMs) with institution-specific prompting can identify protected health information (PHI) that existing de-identification systems miss, improving recall without sacrificing precision. On a dataset of 100 pediatric oncology notes, the best LLM configuration achieved an F1 score of 0.918, outperforming purpose-built systems like Stanford TiDE (0.779). This research addresses a critical gap in de-identification for electronic health records, where institution-specific PHI (e.g., hospital abbreviations, building names) is often missed. It shows that LLMs can serve as a legitimate, adaptable alternative to traditional de-identification systems, potentially improving privacy protection and enabling safer secondary use of clinical data. The study benchmarked eight LLMs against two purpose-built systems and two pattern-based baselines, using three prompt strategies of increasing specificity. Naming the missed categories recovered 79% of them, and discouraging over-redaction restored precision. Notably, no agentic architecture outperformed calibrated single-pass prompting, but LLM outputs surfaced 414 candidate annotation gaps, of which 227 were confirmed as PHI, leading to a final recall of 0.981.

In-context learning (ICL) allows LLMs to adapt to new tasks by providing examples or instructions in the prompt, without retraining. This study leverages ICL to improve PHI detection by incorporating institution-specific categories into the prompt.

This work addresses a key limitation of tensor networks—performing nonlinear operations—thereby expanding their applicability to general-purpose data science and large-scale optimization. The framework operates entirely in the compressed domain, maintaining controlled computational cost. ## [Authorization Before Context: A New Boundary Against Cross-Audience Memory Leakage](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.17148) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces 'authorization before context', a rule that enforces audience membership at the memory-to-context transition in agentic systems, preventing unauthorized facts from entering the prompt. It provides a formal proof and demonstrates on a synthetic Contextual-Integrity suite that no forbidden facts leak into the context. This addresses a critical security and privacy issue in agentic AI systems, where personal language agents might leak information across different audiences. The proposed model-neutral boundary could be widely adopted to enhance trust and safety in AI applications, especially those handling sensitive user data. The rule is anti-monotone: an item is admitted only if every current viewer already belonged to its audience, and it falls back to public when channel metadata is ambiguous. The paper proves that the rule prevents cross-channel recall from leaking to broader audiences and that poisoned memory cannot widen its own audience, ensuring a forbidden fact is absent before the model is called.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 19, 04:00

**Background**: Agentic AI systems use memory to retain context across sessions, but this memory can be a source of data leakage if not properly controlled. The paper addresses the 'memory-to-context' step, where facts from one audience might be included in prompts for another audience, violating contextual integrity. The proposed rule is a formal, model-neutral invariant that can be applied regardless of the underlying AI model.

## [QWM: World Models Boost Q-Learning Sample Efficiency](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.17163) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces QWM, a framework that integrates world models with standard Q-learning to perform test-time search over imagined trajectories, improving sample efficiency and performance in off-policy RL without relying on imagined rollouts for training. It significantly outperforms prior state-of-the-art methods on Robomimic and LIBERO benchmarks. This work addresses a key challenge in model-based RL—compounding bias—by keeping policy and value function training grounded in real transitions while still gaining sample-efficiency benefits from predictive search. It could enable more efficient RL fine-tuning for high-dimensional tasks like real-world robotics, advancing the applicability of RL in complex environments. QWM performs test-time search over imagined trajectories to select high-value actions during both online rollouts and evaluation, while the policy and value function are trained only on real transitions. The method avoids compounding model bias and demonstrates significant improvements on challenging manipulation benchmarks Robomimic and LIBERO.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 19, 04:00

**Background**: Off-policy reinforcement learning (RL) has become increasingly sample-efficient, enabling applications like RL fine-tuning of Vision-Language-Action models. World models predict state changes and offer a further lever for sample efficiency, but prior model-based RL methods often optimize policies on imagined rollouts, which is prone to compounding bias and struggles to scale to high-dimensional problems. Q-learning is a model-free algorithm that learns action values directly from interactions, and integrating world models with it in a grounded manner is a novel approach.

This paper introduces a practitioner framework with six patterns for token optimization and context-window management in multi-agent AI workflows, demonstrating a 60-70% token reduction and cutting cold-load latency to 61-116 seconds from a baseline of 3.5-10.5 minutes in production. This framework addresses the critical challenges of cost and latency in LLM-based multi-agent systems, offering repeatable patterns that can significantly improve efficiency and reliability for practitioners. It bridges the gap between model research and production agent practice, potentially influencing how AI workflows are designed and deployed. The six patterns are context stratification, fetch-once/process-locally architecture, schema-contracted prompts, token-aware fallback chains, semantic caching, and inter-agent communication compression. A controlled study with 2,420 trials across 11 model configurations found that a 50:50 signal/noise context improved relevance accuracy by +0.077 over a 100% high-relevance condition, with a Cohen's d of 0.49.

## [Teach-and-Grow Learning: Agent-Centered Architecture for General Robot Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.17209) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces Teach-and-Grow Learning (TGL), an agent-centered architecture that converts a few successful demonstrations into reusable Skill Blocks, enabling new tasks to be acquired without task-specific policy retraining. It achieves state-of-the-art performance on LIBERO benchmarks and proposes the Teach-and-Grow scaling-law hypothesis. This work addresses the 'retraining tax' in robot learning, a significant bottleneck for deploying general-purpose robots in real-world settings. By enabling continuous learning and reuse of skills, TGL could reduce data requirements and improve generalization, potentially accelerating the adoption of VLA and world-action models in robotics. TGL uses a multimodal agent to turn demonstrations into closed-loop Skill Blocks, stored in a Skill Library, with an Experience Memory tracking success, failure, and repair. The architecture supports agent-directed adaptation and tool selection, and the scaling-law hypothesis suggests that error and teaching demand decrease as power laws of effective reusable experience.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 19, 04:00

**Background**: Vision-language-action (VLA) models and world-action models are recent approaches in robotics that map visual and language inputs to actions, but they often require extensive retraining when encountering novel objects or environments. The 'retraining tax' refers to the recurring cost of collecting new data, updating policies, and regression testing. TGL aims to mitigate this by learning reusable skills from few demonstrations, similar to how humans build on prior knowledge.
