## [Reasoning Effort in LLM APIs: Higher Cost, No Clear Accuracy Gain](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.16956) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new empirical study on arXiv (2608.16956) tested explicit 'high reasoning effort' settings in LLM APIs against omitted effort, using 30 AIME 2026 items with five calls each. The study found a mean cost increase of $0.01031 per call, but no statistically significant accuracy improvement. This research highlights that API buyers pay for a dated contract, not just a model name, and that reasoning-effort settings may not always deliver value. It underscores the need for developers to empirically test cost-performance trade-offs for their specific use cases. The study used a registered paired contrast of Sonnet 5 with explicit high effort versus omitted effort, with a cost contrast of +$0.01031 per call and an accuracy contrast of +0.0133, with a confidence interval that allows up to a 4.67 percentage point gain. The cost per correct answer was $0.08665 for high effort and $0.07662 for omitted effort.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 19, 04:00

**Background**: LLM APIs often allow users to set a 'reasoning effort' parameter, which controls how much computation the model spends on reasoning. Higher effort typically increases cost and latency, but the accuracy benefits are not always clear. This study uses AIME 2026 math problems, a standard benchmark for evaluating reasoning capabilities, to assess the trade-off.

## [FedPref: Federated Preference Learning for Radiology Report Extraction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.16971) ⭐️ 7.0/10

FedPref introduces a federated learning framework that uses frozen public language models to propose alternative JSON extractions, local annotations to rank them, and trains compact Qwen3-8B adapters across hospitals without sharing raw data. On simulated data from six hospitals, it improves client-mean F1 by 2.49 points and worst-site F1 by 9.10 points compared to isolated training. This approach addresses data heterogeneity and privacy constraints in healthcare AI, enabling smaller hospitals with limited data to benefit from collaborative learning without sharing sensitive reports. It demonstrates significant gains for data-scarce sites, potentially improving the accuracy and equity of radiology report extraction across institutions. The framework uses a heterogeneous teacher pool to provide cross-model contrast, preventing collapse when repeated single-model samples are used. On a locked, 400-report manually validated gold test set, FedPref achieves 68.68 F1, while pooled training reaches 71.67, preserving the same ordering.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 19, 04:00

**Background**: Radiology reports are typically free text, but downstream analysis requires structured extraction in a fixed schema. Federated learning allows multiple institutions to collaboratively train models without sharing raw data, which is crucial in healthcare due to privacy regulations. Preference learning involves using rankings of model outputs to align models with human preferences, and adapters are small trainable modules added to frozen base models.

## [SkillEffect: Checked Lowering for Memory-Bounded Agent Tools](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.17007) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces SkillEffect, a checked-lowering runtime that enforces memory bounds for agent tool calls by validating program transformations against source relations and postconditions. It demonstrates that this architecture can reduce peak memory and improve completion under fixed memory caps across six operator families. This addresses a critical safety and efficiency problem in AI/ML systems where language models generate tool-use programs that may exceed memory limits. The approach offers a general, auditable mechanism to enforce memory bounds, potentially improving reliability and enabling more complex agent workflows. SkillEffect uses a plugin architecture where each relation plugin supplies a source recognizer, input-fact extractor, bounded-IR constructor, arena-bound function, and postcondition. The runtime provides checked selection, bounded-VM execution, atomic capacity leasing, and staged publication, and the checker accepts all legal configurations and rejects adversarial proposals.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 19, 04:00

**Background**: Agent Skills allow language models to specify procedural and resource obligations for tool use, but models may generate semantically correct programs that load entire inputs and exceed memory limits. Checked lowering is a technique that validates program transformations before execution, ensuring they meet specified constraints. This paper applies it to memory bounds, using a runtime that checks each proposed lowering against source relations and postconditions.

## [Benchmarking the Benchmarks: Evaluating Automated Safety Benchmarks for SLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.17183) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper evaluates five widely used automated safety benchmark suites across 26 open-source small language models (SLMs) using a unified judging rubric, finding that ambiguous judgments dominate and that LLM-centric benchmarks are insufficient as standalone evidence for SLM safety assessment. This finding is significant because SLMs are increasingly deployed in resource-constrained and privacy-sensitive settings, where safety failures pose real risks. The study highlights a capability-safety confound and shows that aggregate leaderboards are mathematically brittle, which could impact how the community evaluates and trusts SLM safety. The study involved 715,312 prompt-SLM evaluations across five benchmarks and 26 models. Ambiguity rates increase with lexical density, output perplexity, and output length, while decreasing with lexical sophistication, self-coherence, and reply-prompt similarity, revealing a capability-safety confound.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 19, 04:00

**Background**: Small language models (SLMs) are compact versions of large language models (LLMs) designed for efficient deployment on edge devices. Existing safety benchmarks are typically developed for LLMs and may not transfer reliably to SLMs. This paper systematically evaluates the transferability of these benchmarks to SLMs, addressing a critical gap in AI safety evaluation.

## [Explicit State Elicitation Fails to Improve Memory-Policy Classification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.17247) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a controlled audit protocol for memory-policy classification, revealing that explicit state elicitation alone does not significantly improve policy accuracy for Llama-3.3-70B and GPT-OSS-120B, after controlling for dataset shortcuts and prompt bundling. This work highlights the importance of rigorous evaluation methodologies in AI systems, particularly for personalized agents that rely on memory policies. It cautions against overestimating model capabilities based on naive prompting, which is crucial for developing reliable and trustworthy AI. The study uses a 480-example synthetic development set and a frozen 160-example controlled counterfactual set with 40 matched four-way families. It finds that supplying benchmark-associated state labels acts as a label-conditioning diagnostic rather than evidence of an internal mechanism, and that example-level accuracy overstates counterfactual consistency.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 19, 04:00

**Background**: Memory-policy classification in personalized agents involves deciding whether retrieved user memory should be used, ignored, updated, or queried. Dataset shortcuts are spurious correlations that lead to good training performance but poor generalization, while prompt bundling refers to combining multiple changes in prompts, which can confound evaluations. This paper addresses these issues by auditing shortcuts and isolating prompt changes.

## [Logit-Based Energy Scoring Outperforms LLM-as-Judge for Hypothesis Ranking](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.17270) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new paper proposes a logit-based energy scoring method for ranking scientific hypotheses, which uses a language model's intrinsic confidence rather than comparative judgment. In a benchmark across 12 disciplines, this method achieved 33.0% Hit@1 pooled across both scorers, compared to 16.6% for prompted listwise ranking. This work addresses a critical challenge in AI-driven science: evaluating generated hypotheses reliably. By showing that intrinsic model confidence can outperform LLM-as-judge approaches, it offers a promising direction for building trustworthy AI-enabled scientific workflows. The strongest configuration, a 1-billion-parameter model using logit-based energy scoring, reached 53.1% Hit@1, though this was the maximum across 14 model-by-scorer combinations selected post hoc. The study benchmarked seven language models on 1,323 papers, each paired with its hypothesis and fifteen incorrect alternatives.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 19, 04:00

**Background**: Large language models (LLMs) are increasingly used for scientific hypothesis generation, but evaluating these hypotheses is challenging. Existing approaches often use LLMs as judges or rely on semantic similarity, which can favor familiar ideas over novel ones. Logit-based energy scoring is a method that derives a score from the logits (pre-softmax outputs) of a model, reflecting its intrinsic confidence in a given input. This approach is inspired by energy-based out-of-distribution detection, where lower energy indicates higher confidence.

The paper introduces DeAR, a decentralized agentic reasoning framework that replaces centralized protocols with autonomous peer-to-peer collaboration, featuring capability grounding, thought map navigation, and topology updates. Evaluations across 9 multimodal and text-based QA benchmarks show consistent improvements over recent baselines. This work addresses key limitations of centralized agentic systems, such as routing bottlenecks and static role allocation, which are critical for handling complex multimodal queries. By demonstrating that decentralized, adaptive collaboration enhances accuracy, DeAR could influence future designs of multi-agent LLM systems and collaborative AI. DeAR is built on three mechanisms: decentralized capability grounding for query-dependent agent specialization, thought map navigation for targeted peer interactions, and topology update for adaptive error correction. The paper is a preprint with no code released yet, but source code is promised upon acceptance.

## [PlanPO: Group Planning-Aware Policy Optimization for Multi-Turn Agentic LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.17289) ⭐️ 7.0/10

PlanPO introduces a coarse-to-fine advantage signal that differentiates successful trajectories by interaction efficiency, improving planning abilities in multi-turn agentic LLMs. It outperforms GRPO by 27.2% on average across ALFWorld, WebShop, and SciWorld benchmarks. This method addresses the advantage collapse problem in group-relative policy optimization, which limits the training efficiency and planning capabilities of agentic LLMs. By enabling agents to learn generalizable planning behaviors, PlanPO could lead to more efficient and capable AI agents in multi-turn interactive tasks. PlanPO captures relative differences in trajectory-level lengths and turn-level response lengths conditioned on successful trajectories, avoiding degeneration into vanilla length minimization. It incurs negligible additional training cost while outperforming recent powerful baselines.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 19, 04:00

**Background**: Group-relative policy optimization (GRPO) is a reinforcement learning paradigm for training agentic LLMs on multi-turn tasks, where advantages are computed relative to other completions in the same group. However, existing variants often assign identical rewards to all successful trajectories, leading to advantage collapse and performance bottlenecks. PlanPO introduces a coarse-to-fine advantage signal to differentiate trajectories based on efficiency, promoting deliberate planning and generation.

## [LLM Medical Consultation Evaluation Misses the Preformulation Gap](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.17330) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new preprint (arXiv:2608.17330) reveals that LLMs often give premature self-care advice before clarifying a patient's vague concern, and proposes evaluating first-contact behavior directly. In experiments, 9 of 12 baseline case-model cells contained premature advice, dropping to 0 of 12 with an entry-to-care instruction. This highlights a critical blind spot in current LLM medical consultation evaluation, which typically assumes the clinical problem is already clear. Addressing the preformulation gap is essential for safe deployment of LLMs in real-world healthcare, where patients often present vague or minimized concerns. The study evaluated three API models across four physician-authored multi-turn vignettes under baseline and instruction conditions, yielding 24 fixed-script transcripts and 12 adaptive standardized-patient transcripts. The instruction improved sequencing and documentation but did not reliably ensure elicitation of decisive facts.

## [GraphGAN for Adaptive DDoS Detection in Next-Gen Networks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.17352) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces GraphGAN, a Graph-based Generative Adversarial Network that combines k-NN graph construction with a GCN-based discriminator to detect DDoS attacks adaptively. It addresses class imbalance by generating synthetic minority samples and achieves superior performance on four benchmark datasets. This work is significant because it tackles two critical challenges in network security: class imbalance and non-stationarity in DDoS detection. By leveraging graph neural networks and adversarial training, it offers a topology-aware solution that could improve intrusion detection in data-constrained environments, benefiting network operators and security researchers. GraphGAN converts sequential flows into k-nearest neighbor graphs using sliding windows to preserve feature-similarity and temporal dependencies. The generator synthesizes realistic DDoS samples, while a GCN-based discriminator distinguishes real from synthetic data, and a separate GCN classifier performs final detection. Evaluations show superior accuracy, precision, and recall compared to state-of-the-art methods, especially in data-scarce scenarios.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 19, 04:00

**Background**: Distributed Denial-of-Service (DDoS) attacks aim to overwhelm network resources, requiring robust detection mechanisms. Traditional methods often struggle with class imbalance, where attack traffic is rare compared to normal traffic, and with non-stationary traffic patterns. Graph Neural Networks (GNNs) can capture relational structures in data, and Generative Adversarial Networks (GANs) can generate synthetic samples to balance datasets. This paper combines these techniques to create a cognitive detection engine.

## [Task-Aware Harness Provisioning Optimizes LLM Agents for Critical Infrastructure](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.17433) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a task-aware harness provisioning algorithm for LLM agents in mission-critical infrastructure, framing harness configuration as a resource-matching problem. The proposed map-guided escalation method improves accuracy in liquid cooling tasks from 0.652 to 0.715 while using 48% fewer tokens than Reflexion. This work addresses a practical gap in LLM agent deployment for critical systems, where over-provisioning harnesses wastes resources and may reduce efficiency. By optimizing harness configurations per task, it can improve both performance and cost-effectiveness, with implications for AI systems research and real-world infrastructure operations. The algorithm constructs task-to-harness mappings from research literature mining and controlled agent execution, then uses a map-guided escalation strategy that starts with a task-specific harness and expands only after a failed self-check. Evaluation in power grids shows full provision remains accuracy-optimal, but map-based provisioning offers lower-cost alternatives, indicating a domain-dependent accuracy-cost Pareto frontier.

## [Quadratic Depth Dependence Proven Intrinsic in Deep ReLU Regression](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.17434) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper proves that the quadratic depth dependence in the generalization error of deep ReLU regression is intrinsic, closing a gap between existing lower and upper bounds. It constructs a local packing with log-cardinality Ω(L^2 w^2 log w) and shows minimax risk at least of order L^2 w^2 log(w) R^2/n under specific conditions. This result resolves a fundamental question in deep learning theory about how depth affects generalization, providing a tighter characterization for deep ReLU networks. It impacts researchers studying statistical learning theory and may guide architecture design by clarifying the cost of depth. The proof relies on a bias-corrected bounded-coefficient approximation theorem and balanced amplification, where multiplying a depth-D ReLU network by q costs only O(D w^2 q^(1/D)) in layer-sum. The lower bound is derived via Gaussian Fano, and a pseudodimension-based finite-net upper bound gives O-tilde(L^2 w^2 R^2/n) for unbounded Gaussian responses.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 19, 04:00

**Background**: Deep ReLU networks are a popular architecture using rectified linear units, and their generalization properties are studied via variation spaces like the Parhi-Nowak deep-RBV^2 architecture. The layer-sum variation budget controls the complexity of the network, and minimax risk quantifies the best possible worst-case error. This work builds on prior bounds that differed by one factor of depth, aiming to determine the true dependence.

## [SIRLM: Structure-Internalized Rule Language Model for Faithful KG Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.17443) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces SIRLM, a novel framework that couples structural rule generation with faithfulness evaluation to mitigate reasoning evidence perception drift in LLMs for knowledge graph reasoning. It includes a Structure-Internalized Rule Generator (SIRG) with a KG tokenizer and a neuro-symbolic reasoner, and can be integrated into standard LLM training paradigms like SFT and GRPO. This work addresses a critical limitation in LLM-based knowledge graph reasoning: the inconsistency between KG structural context and parametric knowledge, which undermines reasoning faithfulness. By enabling LLMs to anchor to KG-grounded evidence, SIRLM could improve the reliability of AI systems in tasks requiring structured knowledge, such as question answering and fact verification. SIRLM's SIRG incorporates an in-context learning block augmented with a structural relation memory, a KG tokenizer based on structural invariance learning, and a neuro-symbolic reasoner using rule-constrained message propagation. Extensive experiments against 17 state-of-the-art KGR methods on 36 datasets demonstrate significant superiority.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 19, 04:00

**Background**: Knowledge graph reasoning (KGR) aims to discover latent facts by leveraging structural evidence in knowledge graphs. Large language models (LLMs) have shown promise in KGR via in-context learning, but they often suffer from 'reasoning evidence perception drift,' where the model's internal biases override KG constraints, leading to unfaithful reasoning. This paper builds on prior work in faithful reasoning, such as graph-constrained reasoning and FiDeLiS, which also aim to ground LLM reasoning in verifiable KG evidence.

## [SAGE: Self-Evolving Storyboard Skills via Attribution-Guided Rule Evolution](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.17468) ⭐️ 7.0/10

SAGE is a deployed framework that learns, attributes, evolves, and routes directing knowledge from expert demonstrations to automate storyboarding for short dramas. It scored 77.8 on an expert-validated rubric across 18 test episodes, outperforming professional directors (77.1), and was deployed for 14 days on Virtual Film Studio, producing 1,344 narrative group outputs with 87.2% accepted without substantive edits. This addresses a critical bottleneck in automated short drama production by enabling LLMs to acquire, refine, and inject tacit directing knowledge without manual intervention. The significant reduction in authoring time (over 83%) and high acceptance rate demonstrate practical impact, potentially accelerating AI-driven content creation workflows. SAGE derives content-independent rules by contrasting training screenplays with expert storyboards, and uses attribution records to enable targeted rule updates. Evolved rules form scenario packages with a routing index, ensuring each narrative group retrieves only a bounded set of relevant rules. The authors also release PROSE, the first public dataset pairing screenplays with storyboards across 68 episodes.

SAGE addresses these by learning rules from expert demonstrations and evolving them through attribution-guided feedback.

## [When AI Designs AI: Innovation or Imitation?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.17471) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a systematic analysis of whether LLM agents can design AI methods that match or surpass human-designed methods. It finds that agents occasionally achieve state-of-the-art performance (10/72 configurations) but 96.8% of their designs fall within human-derived algorithmic design spaces, with nearly half exactly matching existing human designs. This research addresses a timely question about the creative and innovative capabilities of AI agents in the context of automated machine learning. The findings suggest that while LLM agents can match human performance, they largely imitate rather than innovate, which has implications for the future of AI research and development. The analysis derives task-specific algorithmic design spaces from human-designed methods and maps both human- and agent-designed methods into these spaces, quantifying differences at the module level. The evaluation covers multiple modalities and shows that success does not generalize reliably across tasks or agents.

**Background**: LLM agents are AI systems that use large language models to perform tasks autonomously, often with tool use and reasoning. Automated machine learning (AutoML) aims to automate the design of AI models and methods. This paper explores whether such agents can generate novel algorithmic designs or merely recombine existing human choices.

The paper introduces SGHA, a fully automated, corpus-first agent that discovers research problems using a locally served open-weight 9B language model, avoiding reliance on proprietary black-box models. This addresses critical concerns of transparency, auditability, and data privacy in AI-driven science, as it eliminates the need for proprietary frontier-model APIs. SGHA compares favorably with the AI Scientist-v2 idea formulation module across five machine-learning domains. All LLM-based components run on a locally served open-weight 9B model, and the pipeline outputs assumptions, objectives, success criteria, and remaining ambiguities.

## [TRUSS: Evidence-Guided Framework for Safe Agent Skill Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.17588) ⭐️ 7.0/10

TRUSS is a new framework that generates Agent Skills with both functional and safety validation, using evidence-guided static checks and a controllable execution environment. It achieves 100% precision and recall in vulnerability detection and significantly improves task effectiveness and security rates on multiple benchmarks. This work addresses the critical challenge of ensuring AI agent skills are both effective and safe, which is essential for deploying agents in real-world applications. By providing a method to validate and refine skills automatically, TRUSS could reduce the risk of harmful actions and increase trust in AI agents. TRUSS evaluates skills under nine predefined safety properties and uses a shadow agent in a Controllable Execution Environment with brokered tools and provenance-preserving traces. In experiments, it reduced attack success from 38.71% to 19.35% with GPT-5.5 and from 46.45% to 29.68% with GPT-5.4, with zero attack regression, and raised task effectiveness from 17.11% to 52.94% while increasing the Security rate from 50.80% to 100.00%.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 19, 04:00

**Background**: Agent Skills are reusable natural language procedures with executable resources that allow AI agents to acquire task-specific capabilities without model adaptation. However, evaluating a skill solely from its artifact or final outcome can miss unintended actions and side effects. TRUSS addresses this by combining static checks with dynamic execution in a controlled environment, providing evidence-based validation.

This paper systematically studies how static corruptions and sequential perturbations affect confidence-based routing in model cascades for image classification, identifying three failure modes and showing that robustness can be improved while maintaining energy efficiency. As AI energy consumption becomes a critical concern, model cascades offer a promising solution, but their real-world deployment depends on reliability under distribution shifts. This work highlights the need to evaluate cascades beyond clean accuracy, guiding the design of more robust and sustainable AI systems. The authors select a cascade at the Pareto optimum of accuracy, routing quality, and energy, achieving up to a 10-fold reduction in CO2 emissions. They identify three failure modes: static corruptions can break the routing signal or degrade both models, while sequential perturbations can suppress deferral, leading to stable but unreliable predictions.

AutoResearch is a new two-stage autonomous research system that separates Idea Generation from Idea Execution, using multi-model generation and cross-review to produce grounded research plans, and independent evidence-based review before accepting conclusions. In tests on the RSICD benchmark, it improved mean Recall from 32.84 to 34.69 while recording only 5 audit-confirmed issue events, compared to 11-27 for other systems. This addresses a critical flaw in AI-driven research: automation alone does not guarantee scientific validity. By grounding both idea formation and experimental conclusions, AutoResearch could make autonomous discovery more reliable and trustworthy, impacting fields like cross-modal retrieval and systems optimization. The system uses coordinated agents to decompose plans into experiments, iteratively implement and diagnose them, and employs evidence-based review to decide whether to continue, revise, or terminate research directions. It demonstrated effectiveness across cross-modal retrieval, systems optimization, and benchmark-driven machine learning, with the RSICD improvement as a concrete example.

## [EvoTS-Agent: Self-Evolving LLM Agent for Financial Change Point Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.17933) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces EvoTS-Agent, a validation-guided self-evolving LLM agent that autonomously performs change-point detection in financial time series. It uses curated exploratory data analysis and three evolutionary operators—Revision, Alternative Strategy, and Recombination—to evolve detection pipelines, achieving a 100% execution success rate across benchmark datasets. This work addresses the challenge of non-stationary financial time series where no single algorithm works consistently, reducing reliance on expert-driven model selection. It demonstrates the potential of LLM agents to automate and adapt machine learning pipelines in finance, which could improve scalability and adaptability in quantitative analysis. EvoTS-Agent evolves experiment trajectories through three operators: Revision exploits the current best solution, Alternative Strategy explores new directions when progress stagnates, and Recombination synthesizes evidence from high-performing trajectories. Experiments on four benchmark datasets show it consistently outperforms existing LLM-based agents while maintaining a 100% execution success rate across all evaluated backbone LLMs.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 19, 04:00

**Background**: Change-point detection in financial time series is challenging due to non-stationary and heterogeneous statistical properties, often requiring expert-driven model selection and tuning. LLM agents are AI systems that use large language models to perform tasks like data analysis, and evolutionary operators are techniques inspired by natural selection to iteratively improve solutions. This paper combines these concepts to create a self-evolving agent for automated change-point detection.

## [FlightLLM: Prior-Guided Semantic LLM Approach for Flight Safety Event Explanation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.18017) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces FlightLLM, a novel prior-guided semantic LLM-based approach for interpretable flight safety event analysis. It addresses challenges like modal inconsistency and limited domain knowledge by combining feature engineering, semantic discretization, CatBoost as a statistical expert, contrastive few-shot learning, and structured prompts. This work demonstrates a practical application of LLMs to a critical real-world domain, potentially improving flight safety by providing clear, interpretable explanations of risk events. It also offers a framework that could be adapted to other domains facing similar challenges of data scarcity and domain knowledge gaps. The approach was evaluated on a dataset of 704 real-world A320 flight samples, focusing on hard landing events. The results show competitive classification performance while generating direct and reasonable explanations for event causes.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 19, 04:00

**Background**: Flight safety analysis traditionally relies on detecting risk events from flight data and interpreting their causes, often using explainable AI techniques like feature importance maps that require significant domain expertise. Large Language Models (LLMs) offer promise for generating natural language explanations, but face challenges such as modal inconsistency between numerical data and language, limited classification ability, and scarcity of task-specific data. FlightLLM addresses these by converting numerical patterns into qualitative descriptions, incorporating a statistical classifier for prior guidance, and embedding aviation knowledge through structured prompts.

## [Delegation Asymmetry in Agentic Recommender Systems: Measuring Two-Sided Receptivity in Online Dating](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.18058) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper empirically measures the asymmetry between users' willingness to delegate conversations to AI agents and their receptivity to receiving agent-mediated communication, using two large-scale surveys of active users of a major dating platform (N=2,894 and N=2,617). It develops a latent-variable measurement model showing that willingness to send and receive agent communication are distinct constructs (rho=0.92, Delta BIC=52), and quantifies a systematic delegation asymmetry where deploying one's own agent requires lower receptivity than engaging a counterpart's agent. This research addresses a critical yet underexplored condition for the viability of agentic recommender systems: users must accept both delegating and receiving agent-mediated communication. The findings have direct implications for designing agentic systems in matching platforms and beyond, highlighting the need for receptivity-aware matchmaking and disclosure mechanisms. The model uses graded response models with latent regression and shows partial measurement invariance across languages. Under a random-pairing counterfactual, only 4-13% of directed dyads combine agent deployment with receiver engagement, and a reciprocity requirement cuts interaction volume by half or more, while routing agent contacts on receive receptivity triples per-contact engagement (AUC 0.88, 3.1x quartile lift).

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 19, 04:00

**Background**: Agentic recommender systems are an emerging paradigm where autonomous, often LLM-powered agents drive adaptive, interactive, multi-step decision making over extended user interactions. In such systems, users may delegate conversations to agents, but the viability depends on both sending and receiving agent-mediated communication. This paper uses latent-variable measurement models, such as graded response models, to quantify user receptivity and asymmetry, which are commonly used in psychometrics to measure unobservable traits.

## [QuantumNovelty: AI Agent Audits Quantum Papers and Patents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.16900) ⭐️ 7.0/10

QuantumNovelty, an open-source skill-orchestrating language agent, generates and reviews quantum-computing papers, Pareto-front ansatz candidates, and patent drafts using simulated referee and patent-examiner panels. It introduces deterministic audit gates—strict Pareto domination, numerical recomputation, Wilson small-sample intervals, and a cross-vendor consensus guard—to constrain claims, with all model calls logged for cost transparency. This framework addresses the growing need for auditable and reproducible AI-generated scientific output, particularly in quantum computing where verification is challenging. It offers a cost-transparent decision-support tool that could help researchers, journals, and patent offices screen submissions, though it is not a replacement for human peer review. In a planted adversarial corpus, the deterministic gates caught every overclaim with no false positives. A first deployment on six manuscripts and one granted patent cost about $24, and the panels were directionally more conservative than the public acceptance record, though the authors make no accuracy claim against human experts.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 19, 04:00

**Background**: Language-model agents are increasingly used to produce scientific results, but their outputs often lack rigorous verification. QuantumNovelty builds on the concept of skill-orchestrating agents, similar to hardware-bring-up systems, but applies them to the publication and patent domain. The deterministic gates use techniques like Pareto dominance (comparing multi-objective solutions) and Wilson intervals (for small-sample statistics) to ensure claims are falsifiable and reproducible.

## [ComNetX: A Solver-Agnostic Hierarchical Framework for Dynamic Community Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.16906) ⭐️ 7.0/10

ComNetX introduces a solver-agnostic hierarchical adaptation framework for local dynamic community detection, which maintains a multi-level community state, expands the updated region, closes it over affected communities, and contracts these communities into compact local instances. In paired runs on the largest real graph, it achieves a 41.9x speedup for Local Leiden while keeping final modularity within 0.006 of full-snapshot recomputation. This framework addresses a significant gap in dynamic community detection by providing a general solution that works with various solvers, potentially reducing computational costs for large-scale network analysis. It could benefit applications in social network analysis, biology, and other fields where graphs evolve over time. ComNetX is evaluated on six real networks, longer real-data streams for topology-based backends, and controlled dynamic stochastic block model stress streams. The combined protocols identify regimes where locality breaks down and a full refresh is preferable, indicating the framework's adaptability.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 19, 04:00

**Background**: Dynamic community detection aims to identify communities in evolving networks. Traditional approaches either recompute from scratch on each snapshot, which is expensive, or use solver-specific dynamic updates that may not generalize. ComNetX offers a solver-agnostic approach that preserves solver context while localizing computation to changed parts of the graph.

## [Sparse Coverage: Semantic Centers for Patent Prior-Art Retrieval](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.16918) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Sparse Coverage is an unsupervised retrieval framework that maps local span embeddings to sparse centers selected via a coverage-oriented k-center objective, enabling inverted-index retrieval. On CLEF-IP 2013, it matches or exceeds dense patent encoders in document-level recall and remains competitive for passage-level retrieval. This work addresses a critical limitation of dense retrieval in patent search, where single-vector representations may compress multiple technical components. By combining local semantic evidence with sparse inverted-index search, it offers an effective first-stage retrieval approach that could improve recall and efficiency in patent prior-art search. The framework uses a coverage-oriented k-center objective to select centers, and spans activate nearby centers to produce sparse representations. Experiments on CLEF-IP 2013 show that Sparse Coverage matches or exceeds strong dense patent encoders in several configurations for document-level recall, while remaining competitive for passage-level retrieval.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 19, 04:00

**Background**: Patent prior-art retrieval is a recall-oriented search task over long, structured technical documents. Dense retrieval improves semantic matching but may compress multiple technical components into a single embedding. Sparse representations, on the other hand, are compatible with inverted-index retrieval and offer efficiency and transparency. CLEF-IP 2013 is a standard benchmark for patent retrieval, including tasks like passage retrieval from claims.

## [CARA: Cognitive Adaptive Recommendation Agent Framework](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.16919) ⭐️ 7.0/10

CARA introduces a cognitively inspired recommendation framework that models user decisions through intuitive affective and deliberate rational processes in two stages: candidate filtering and dual-perspective decision modeling. It also proposes a boundary-aware KTO strategy to enhance preference signal density. This framework addresses a key limitation in existing recommendation systems by explicitly modeling the cognitive processes behind user decisions, potentially leading to more accurate and context-aware recommendations. It represents a meaningful advancement over semantic matching and loosely structured agent workflows, with relative improvements of up to 10.15% over baselines. CARA is evaluated on three Amazon Reviews domains, achieving the best performance on most metrics. The boundary-aware KTO strategy focuses on instructions the model can solve occasionally but not consistently, increasing the density of informative preference signals.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 19, 04:00

**Background**: Dual process theory in cognitive psychology posits that thought arises from two different processes: an implicit, automatic process and an explicit, controlled process. Existing recommendation systems often rely on semantic matching or end-to-end generation without explicitly modeling how user preferences are processed. CARA leverages this theory to structure recommendation as a two-stage decision-making process, integrating large language models with cognitive modeling.

## [EMAN: Optimization-Driven Capacity Growth in Multi-Task Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.16930) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces EMAN (Emergent Modular Atomic Network), a framework that starts from a single-path network and grows independent paths only when persistent optimization evidence appears, using latent relative phases to expose growth directions without instantiating a second path. It validates the approach on controlled rank settings, PASCAL-Context, and NYUv2, achieving improved performance at competitive computational cost. This work addresses a fundamental question in multi-task learning: whether capacity can be grown adaptively based on optimization signals rather than predefined structures or task boundaries. If successful, it could lead to more efficient and flexible neural architectures that allocate capacity only when needed, potentially reducing computational waste and improving generalization across tasks. EMAN uses latent relative phases to expose an antisymmetric growth direction without instantiating a second path, and monitors multiple decision signals during training to convert local optimization evidence into structural decisions. It materializes two equal-capacity independent paths only after certification, and adaptively allocates shared and task-specific representation capacity.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 19, 04:00

**Background**: Multi-task learning (MTL) aims to solve multiple tasks simultaneously by exploiting commonalities and differences across tasks. Existing MTL methods include hard sharing, multiple paths or experts, adaptive sharing, and dynamic expansion, but their capacity changes are often constrained by predefined structures or triggered by task boundaries and conflict signals. EMAN proposes an optimization-driven approach that grows capacity based on persistent optimization evidence, which is a novel direction in this field.

## [Fairness Failures in Generative Models Are an Evaluation Problem](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.16974) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This position paper argues that fairness failures in generative models are fundamentally an evaluation problem and proposes Fairness Cards, a standardized reporting artifact that makes evaluation choices explicit, including prompt families, counterfactual protocols, metrics, and refusal handling. This matters because current fairness evaluations in generative models are rarely comparable or actionable, hindering progress in addressing societal inequalities. Standardized reporting could improve reproducibility and accountability, benefiting researchers, developers, and policymakers in AI ethics. The paper diagnoses recurring empirical and conceptual failure modes in current practice and motivates a shift from ad-hoc bias checks to standardized, generative-specific evaluation. It concludes with additional recommendations towards a paradigm shift in evaluation standards, and a project page is available at https://mariiavladimirova.github.io/fairness-cards.

**Background**: Generative models, such as large language models and image generators, have advanced rapidly but often reflect or amplify societal biases, harming marginalized groups. Fairness evaluation in this context is challenging because there are no standardized protocols, leading to incomparable results across studies. The paper proposes Fairness Cards as a minimal reporting artifact to address this gap, similar to model cards but focused on fairness evaluation choices.

## [PXDepth: Pixel-Space Modeling for Structure-Preserving Monocular Depth Estimation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.16984) ⭐️ 7.0/10

PXDepth introduces a novel architecture that separates global context modeling using a large-patch ViT from pixel-level depth prediction using Context-Modulated Pixel Transformer blocks, improving structure preservation in monocular depth estimation. The model demonstrates strong zero-shot performance across diverse benchmarks while maintaining efficiency. This work addresses a known limitation in monocular depth estimation—preserving fine-grained structures and object boundaries—which is critical for applications like autonomous driving and robotics. By decoupling global context from pixel-level prediction, it offers a new design paradigm that could influence future depth estimation models. The architecture uses a large-patch ViT for global context and a pixel-space predictor with Context-Modulated Pixel Transformer blocks to maintain high-resolution spatial representations. The model achieves competitive global depth accuracy while preserving fine structures, and the code and model are publicly available.

Recent methods often use large-patch Vision Transformers (ViTs) for global context and convolutional decoders for upsampling, but this combination can lose pixel-level cues. PXDepth addresses this by separating global context modeling from pixel-level prediction, using a pixel-space predictor to preserve fine structures.

## [Cross-Model Memory Transfer: Reader Adaptation Key](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.17050) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper investigates whether frozen hashed memory or the target-side reader is more critical when transferring engram-style memory across different LLM backbones. It introduces cross-model frozen-memory extraction, where a memory trained on a source model is frozen and attached to a different target model, with only a lightweight reader trained. This research addresses a fundamental question in LLM knowledge integration: whether memory content or reader adaptation is more important for cross-model reuse. The findings suggest that engram-style memory can serve as a reusable external knowledge artifact, potentially enabling more flexible and efficient knowledge transfer across models. Ablations show that learned memory content and correct addressing both matter, but the transferred table becomes useful only through a reader aligned to the target model. In downstream question answering tasks, a dual-layer, four-branch reader nearly closes the gap between same-model and cross-model reuse, achieving an average score of 38.8 under a controlled evaluation protocol.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 19, 04:00

**Background**: Engram-style hashed memory stores learned information in an external, addressable table, consumed through a small learned reader, occupying a middle regime between non-parametric retrieval and parametric adaptation. This approach aims to combine the flexibility of external knowledge with the efficiency of parametric methods, but its transferability across different model backbones has been unclear.

## [ICD-Deepresearch: A DeepResearch Workflow for Clinical Code Forecasting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.17075) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces ICD-Deepresearch, a DeepResearch workflow that combines structured EHR foundation models (SparseEHR) with language models (GPT-5) and medical search to forecast next-encounter ICD codes. It achieves patient-averaged precision/recall of 24.60/35.09% on MIMIC-III and 25.14/48.32% on MIMIC-IV, with physician-rated document usefulness of 51% and 68% respectively. This work demonstrates a novel integration of predictive foundation models with research-style workflows, potentially improving clinical decision support and coding accuracy. It also highlights the value of evidence-grounded reasoning in medical AI, which could influence future systems for diagnosis forecasting and documentation. The workflow uses a two-stage candidate generation: SparseEHR provides an EHR Prior for two bounded research expansion rounds, while GPT-5 Direct Forecast offers complementary candidates. Final selection validates, deduplicates, and jointly ranks both paths, and a separate module writes rationales without altering predictions. The system outperforms standalone GPT-5 web search and Medical Deep Research in physician-rated usefulness.

EHR foundation models like SparseEHR capture temporal patterns, while language models generate flexible hypotheses. DeepResearch workflows automate multi-stage research processes using LLM-based planning and tool orchestration, integrating retrieval and evidence synthesis.

## [SLM-Based GNSS Spoofing Detection Using Structured Driving Narratives](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.17092) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a small language model (SLM)-based framework that converts driving states from GNSS and other sensors into structured semantic narratives for detecting and classifying GNSS spoofing attacks in autonomous vehicles. The SLMs achieve performance comparable to large language models (LLMs) with an average accuracy of 96.99%, while requiring lower inference latency and less GPU memory. GNSS spoofing poses a critical security threat to autonomous vehicles, and this work demonstrates that resource-efficient SLMs can effectively detect such attacks, making real-time deployment on vehicular platforms feasible. This could lower the barrier for implementing robust spoofing defenses in production AVs, addressing a key safety concern in the industry. The framework evaluates five attack classes: no attack, overshoot attack, stopped attack, turn-by-turn attack, and wrong-turn attack. It was also tested on geographically unseen field data from Clemson, South Carolina, achieving high efficacy, and the SLMs showed advantages in computational efficiency and resource utilization over LLMs.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 19, 04:00

**Background**: Autonomous vehicles rely on GNSS for positioning, but spoofed signals can cause incorrect vehicle states. Small language models (SLMs) are compact AI models with fewer parameters (typically under 40 billion) that can run on resource-constrained devices, unlike large language models (LLMs) which require massive computational resources. This paper leverages SLMs to compare vehicle behaviors from GNSS and other sensors, converting them into narratives for detection.

## [Expected Free Energy as Information Constraint on Bethe Lagrangian](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.17167) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper reformulates active inference's expected free energy as a constrained Bethe Lagrangian, enabling message passing inference while maintaining the epistemic drive. The authors show that for a specific KKT multiplier value, the stationary point recovers the expected free energy solution. This work bridges active inference and message passing algorithms, potentially enabling more efficient and scalable implementations in neuroscience and AI. It offers a principled way to maintain exploration-exploitation balance in planning, which could impact fields like robotics and decision-making. The constrained Bethe Lagrangian imposes an information constraint requiring mutual information between future observations, states, and parameters given actions to be at least as large as the entropy of the goal prior. The paper analyzes the KKT multiplier across inactive, interior, and saturated regimes, and compares the constrained Bethe agent against EFE and Q-MDP on three tasks.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 19, 04:00

**Background**: Active inference is a Bayesian framework that unifies perception and action by minimizing variational free energy. The expected free energy (EFE) is a central quantity in active inference, but it lacks a Kullback-Leibler structure due to the expectation over future outcomes, which complicates message passing. The Bethe free energy is a functional used in approximate inference, often optimized via belief propagation. The Karush-Kuhn-Tucker (KKT) conditions generalize Lagrange multipliers to handle inequality constraints in optimization.

## [LLM Legal Reasoning on ECtHR Cases Found Shallow](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.17168) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new study evaluates OpenAI GPT 5.4 on legal case forecasting using European Court of Human Rights (ECtHR) cases, finding that the model produces structurally complete but substantively shallow analyses. The study also reveals that LLM-as-a-Judge evaluations are internally consistent but weakly aligned with human annotations. This research highlights significant limitations of current LLMs in high-stakes legal reasoning, cautioning against over-reliance on automated evaluation and task accuracy as proxies for reasoning quality. It has implications for legal tech and AI evaluation practices, urging more rigorous human-in-the-loop validation. The study used an expert-curated prompt that led to more comprehensive reasoning but did not improve prediction accuracy compared to other settings. The authors urge the community not to rely solely on LLM-based evaluation and to avoid using task accuracy as a proxy for reasoning quality.

The European Court of Human Rights (ECtHR) handles cases related to the European Convention on Human Rights, and its decisions are a common benchmark for legal AI research.

## [RuntimeGuard-AI: Durable Signed Receipts for AI Audit Evidence](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.17176) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces RuntimeGuard-AI, a research prototype that provides durable, Ed25519-signed receipts for AI policy decisions, with explicit synchronization boundaries and Merkle-based attestation. It achieves 27,193 requests/s with 141.9 microseconds median latency on an Apple M4 Pro at four worker threads. This addresses a critical gap in AI auditability by making audit evidence durable and verifiable, which is essential for AI governance and compliance. It provides a measured durability-latency trade-off, offering a practical path for integrating auditability into AI systems without assuming a 'free' asynchronous path. The system binds each policy decision to the exact policy source, commits records at caller-selected synchronization boundaries, and returns signed receipts indicating whether the boundary completed. After restart, it validates framed records, manifests, shard placement, sequence continuity, and replay identity, and groups committed records into chained, signed Merkle epochs.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 19, 04:00

**Background**: AI audit records are useful only if their durability and trust boundaries are explicit. Traditional approaches may return a guarded decision before any durable write, minimizing latency but risking evidence loss on crash. RuntimeGuard-AI rebuilds around this constraint, using cryptographic receipts and Merkle trees to provide verifiable evidence. Ed25519 is a digital signature scheme used for signing receipts, and Merkle trees enable efficient and privacy-preserving attestation.

## [TSFT: Task Specialization Fine-Tuning for Sample-Efficient Contextual RL](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.17180) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces Task Specialization Fine-Tuning (TSFT), a novel framework for contextual reinforcement learning that fine-tunes a pretrained policy across task regions under a constrained budget. TSFT uses a parametric model to predict fine-tuning performance and solves the budget allocation problem via integer linear programming. TSFT addresses the sample inefficiency and heterogeneous returns in contextual RL, aligning with the modern pretrain-finetune paradigm. It demonstrates significant improvements in task coverage across diverse domains, potentially influencing future RL research and applications. The framework was validated on combinatorial optimization, continuous control, and LLM fine-tuning, outperforming baselines and approaching oracle performance. It introduces a principled approach to budget allocation, which is a critical challenge in multi-task fine-tuning.

**Background**: Contextual Reinforcement Learning (CRL) generalizes classical RL by considering a context space of related tasks, where each context defines a separate MDP. Traditional approaches either train a single multi-task policy or multiple policies from scratch, but TSFT proposes a unified pretrain-finetune paradigm to improve sample efficiency and task specialization.

## [Graphectory Viewer: Interactive Tool for Agent Trajectory Analysis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.17195) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Graphectory Viewer is a newly released open-source web-based tool that enables interactive, process-centric analysis of software-agent trajectories. It transforms heterogeneous raw trajectories into phase-aware graphs, supporting multiple agent frameworks and providing features like node-level inspection, search, filtering, and Sankey-style summaries. This tool addresses the growing need to analyze agent trajectories beyond final outcomes, enabling researchers to identify behavioral patterns, compare successful and failed runs, and analyze large trajectory corpora. It is valuable for the AI/ML community as it supports reproducibility and further research in understanding software agents. Graphectory Viewer builds on the Graphectory representation, which maps low-level agent actions into semantic phases and graph structures. It is released as an open-source artifact with documentation, precomputed graphs, and a large-scale trajectory corpus, and it supports trajectories from multiple agent frameworks.

## [Agentic Vision-Language Framework for Structural Plan-to-Model Conversion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.17237) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces the first framework that applies an agentic vision-language layer to structural component detection and model drafting from framing-plan PDFs, without task-specific detector training or fine-tuning. The framework combines a deterministic stage for primitive extraction and scale estimation with a guarded agentic stage for typed corrections. This work addresses a labor-intensive and error-prone task in structural engineering, potentially automating the conversion of structural plans into editable finite-element model drafts. It demonstrates a novel integration of deterministic geometry with agentic AI, which could influence broader applications in CAD conversion and engineering workflows. The framework estimates scale within 0.1% of the generator reference for every drawing, and achieves recall/precision scores of 0.922/0.997 for columns, 0.886/0.990 for beams, 1.000/1.000 for walls and braces, and 1.000/0.964 for openings. The evaluation uses a held-out half of an author-generated benchmark of 100 plans, but shares the development generator, so it excludes independently drafted plans and raster evaluation.

**Background**: Structural framing plans are technical drawings that show the layout of structural components like columns, beams, walls, and braces. Converting these plans into finite-element models is traditionally done manually, which is time-consuming and prone to errors. Existing automated systems often rely on task-specific trained neural detectors, while language-model agents in structural engineering typically operate on text or model data rather than the drawings themselves. This paper proposes a hybrid approach that combines deterministic geometry processing with an agentic vision-language model to improve accuracy and reduce manual effort.

This paper proposes using qGaussian distributions, derived from Tsallis entropy maximization, as a robust alternative to Gaussian distributions in sparse learning, and introduces a novel framework adapting the Hager-Zhang conjugate gradient algorithm for efficient optimization. This work addresses critical limitations of Gaussian assumptions in modeling correlated and heterogeneous data, which are common in biostatistics and other fields, potentially improving robustness and efficiency in sparse learning applications. The paper re-derives the multivariate probability density function from Tsallis entropy maximization for correlated data, and applies the Hager-Zhang conjugate gradient algorithm to composite optimization problems in sparse learning, ensuring numerical stability and efficiency.

## [LiftXR: Geometry-Guided Bi-planar X-ray to CT Reconstruction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.17255) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces LiftXR, a novel framework that explicitly reconstructs 3D anatomical layout from bi-planar X-rays to guide CT volume reconstruction, achieving state-of-the-art results on two public datasets. This work addresses the severely ill-posed problem of sparse-view CT reconstruction, which is crucial for reducing radiation exposure in medical imaging. By separating spatial layout recovery from intensity estimation, LiftXR could improve the accuracy and clinical utility of CT reconstruction from limited X-ray views. LiftXR interleaves a layout lifter and an anatomical parser to iteratively refine the anatomical layout and intensity calibration. The framework consistently outperforms recent methods on public datasets and shows improved downstream segmentation performance, indicating better anatomical fidelity.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 19, 04:00

**Background**: X-ray imaging is modeled as the projection of a volumetric attenuation field, where each measurement records accumulated attenuation along a ray path. Reconstructing CT from few X-ray views is ill-posed because depth information is collapsed, making 3D locations and intensities entangled. Bi-planar X-ray imaging, such as EOS, provides two simultaneous views, which can help recover spatial layout. Geometry-guided deep learning techniques have been explored for CT reconstruction, but LiftXR explicitly incorporates layout recovery into the reconstruction process.

## [Nonadaptive Robust Output Regulation for Nonlinear Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.17262) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a nonadaptive control framework for robust output regulation in general nonlinear systems with arbitrarily high relative degree, combining input-driven filters, a generic internal model, and recursive backstepping to achieve global asymptotic regulation without linear parameterization. This work extends robust output regulation to a broader class of nonlinear systems, removing the reliance on linear parameterization and Lyapunov functions with nonpositive derivatives, which are common limitations in adaptive methods. It provides explicit design inequalities, making the approach practical for complex or partially known systems, potentially impacting control engineering applications. The design recasts the regulation problem as robust input-to-state stabilization of an augmented error system, and requires standard assumptions on the exosystem (purely imaginary and simple eigenvalues) and a minimum-phase input-to-state stability condition on internal dynamics. The effectiveness is demonstrated on a benchmark controlled Duffing system.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 19, 04:00

**Background**: Output regulation aims to make a system's output track a reference or reject disturbances generated by an exosystem. The internal model principle embeds a model of the exosystem into the controller to achieve robust regulation. Backstepping is a recursive nonlinear control technique that stabilizes cascaded subsystems, while input-driven filters estimate system states or errors. Traditional adaptive methods often require linear parameterization, which this paper avoids.

Irregular time series forecasting is critical in healthcare and meteorology, and existing methods suffer from bias and limited adaptability. The debiased neural basis-function response mechanism uses importance sampling to correct asymptotic bias, while neural networks parameterize basis functions for adaptability. The multi-scale decomposition module uses average pooling, and a dual-branch decoder is employed for forecasting. Code is available at https://github.com/hnu-vis/DNBNet.

## [ADAPT: Disentangling Robust Features to Combat Overfitting in Vision-Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.17306) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces ADAPT (Adversarial Disentangled Prompt Tuning), a novel prompt tuning framework that uses a dual-prompt mechanism with a target prompt and decoy prompts to disentangle robust features from pseudo-robust shortcuts, mitigating robust generalization overfitting in vision-language models. This work addresses a critical limitation in adversarial prompt tuning for vision-language models, where existing methods suffer from robust generalization overfitting, degrading performance on unseen classes. By providing a theoretical guarantee and empirical improvements, ADAPT could enhance the reliability of VLMs in safety-critical applications. ADAPT employs a target prompt and a pool of decoy prompts; decoys entrap diverse pseudo-robust features while the target prompt is constrained to be orthogonal to decoys in embedding space. The paper provides an analysis showing that the orthogonal loss bounds the effect of pseudo-robust feature shifts on unseen classes, yielding a testing error guarantee. Code is available on GitHub.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 19, 04:00

**Background**: Vision-language models (VLMs) like CLIP are often fine-tuned via prompt tuning to adapt to downstream tasks. Adversarial prompt tuning aims to improve robustness against adversarial examples, but it can lead to robust overfitting, where performance on unseen classes degrades as training progresses. This overfitting is attributed to the learning of pseudo-robust features—shortcuts that do not generalize. ADAPT builds on the concept of feature disentanglement to separate robust from non-robust features.

## [ORPA: Online Residual Policy Adaptation for Real-Time Robot Manipulation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.17323) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces ORPA, a framework that adds a lightweight feedback-conditioned module to a pretrained policy like ACT, enabling real-time residual adjustments in joint space without retraining. It is evaluated on the ALOHA platform for precision-sensitive tasks, showing improved success rates and recovery from perturbations. This addresses a key limitation of imitation learning—sensitivity to errors and distribution shifts—by enabling online correction without expensive retraining. It could make robot policies more robust and practical for real-world deployment, benefiting robotics and learning-based control communities. ORPA operates directly in joint space, predicting residual adjustments conditioned on human feedback, and does not modify the base policy parameters. The framework is validated on the ALOHA platform, demonstrating improvements over baseline policies and rule-based inverse kinematics corrections.

**Background**: Imitation learning policies like ACT (Action Chunking with Transformers) are effective for fine-grained manipulation but often fail under small execution errors or distribution shifts. Traditional fixes require dataset aggregation and full retraining, which is computationally heavy. Residual policy learning is a technique where a small corrective module adjusts actions of a base policy, and ORPA applies this in an online, feedback-driven manner.

## [Fair-ASR: A New Protocol for Fair Black-Box Jailbreak Evaluation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.17360) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces Fair-ASR, an evaluation protocol that uses shared target-call budgets to fairly compare black-box jailbreak attacks. Re-evaluating 11 attacks, the authors find that rankings shift across budgets and propose ReCode, a budget-efficient attack achieving 85% ASR on GPT-5 with only 7.19 attacker calls per request. This work addresses a critical gap in LLM safety evaluation by providing a fair comparison basis, which is essential for accurately assessing attack effectiveness. The findings that rankings change across budgets and simple methods remain competitive could reshape how the community evaluates jailbreak attacks and guide future safety research. Fair-ASR uses target calls as a directly observable and method-agnostic comparison axis while tracking attacker calls separately for efficiency analysis. ReCode combines desensitization rewriting with two low-cost primitives identified by Fair-ASR, achieving high ASR under a budget of 20 target calls.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 19, 04:00

**Background**: Jailbreak attacks aim to bypass LLM safety measures, and attack success rate (ASR) is commonly used to evaluate them. However, ASR often ignores attack budgets, leading to unfair comparisons. Existing compute-aware evaluations reduce resources to FLOPs, which is hard to estimate for black-box models. Fair-ASR addresses this by using target calls as a shared budget.

## [NSPER: Integrating Novelty and Surprise for Efficient Image-Based RL](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.17373) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces NSPER and NSPER+R, methods that integrate novelty and surprise signals for experience prioritization and exploration in image-based reinforcement learning, improving training efficiency and convergence speed on DeepMind Control Suite tasks. Sample efficiency is a central challenge in image-based RL, and this work addresses it by combining two complementary signals, potentially leading to faster learning in complex visual environments. This could benefit applications in robotics, autonomous driving, and game playing where visual inputs are prevalent. NSPER uses novelty to capture underrepresented states and surprise to expose gaps in the agent's understanding, while NSPER+R further integrates these signals as intrinsic rewards to jointly improve replay quality and exploration. Experiments were conducted on DeepMind Control Suite tasks, showing improvements over existing methods.

The paper introduces SemComp-Bench, a benchmark for evaluating semantic task completion in video generation, along with SemComp-Data, a dataset covering six domains. It also proposes a four-stage curation pipeline and an evaluation protocol using a vision-language model to report Outcome Achievement (OA) and Generation Reliability (GR) scores. This benchmark addresses a gap in video generation evaluation by focusing on outcome-oriented semantic grounding rather than just appearance consistency. It provides a standardized way to assess whether generated videos achieve intended task outcomes, which is crucial for advancing practical applications like instructional videos and embodied AI. The dataset includes reference images, detailed and brief instructions, and outcome-centric video clips across six domains. The evaluation protocol uses a VLM to answer structured binary questions, and experiments show that current video generation models struggle to achieve intended outcomes while maintaining semantic grounding.

## [Energy-Aware Knowledge Distillation Challenges FLOPs as Energy Metric](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.17515) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces an energy-aware knowledge distillation approach using many-objective optimization and energy-surrogate models to directly estimate CPU and GPU energy consumption during distillation. It demonstrates that FLOPs is not always a reliable indicator of energy consumption, and that using energy surrogates can reduce inference energy by up to 90% and memory usage by 86% with modest accuracy trade-offs. This work addresses the growing concern of energy consumption in large language models (LLMs) applied to software engineering tasks, which is critical for sustainability and deployment on consumer hardware. By challenging the common use of FLOPs as a proxy for energy, it could lead to more accurate energy reporting and more efficient model compression techniques across the industry. The study uses the Morph methodology, a many-objective optimization-based distillation approach, and extends it with energy-surrogate models that estimate CPU and GPU energy consumption during optimization. Experiments are conducted on clone detection and vulnerability prediction tasks, and also applied to generative code summarization using CodeT5+.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 19, 04:00

**Background**: Knowledge distillation (KD) is a model compression technique where a smaller 'student' model learns to mimic a larger 'teacher' model, transferring capabilities while reducing computational cost. FLOPs (Floating Point Operations) are commonly used to report computational cost, but recent research suggests they may not correlate well with actual energy consumption, especially on modern hardware. Many-objective optimization handles multiple conflicting objectives, such as accuracy and energy, to find Pareto-optimal solutions.

## [CoAL-RAG: Complexity-Aware Legal RAG Method](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.17536) ⭐️ 7.0/10

CoAL-RAG introduces a complexity-aware legal retrieval-augmented generation method that adaptively routes retrieval strategies based on question complexity, using a multi-dimensional evaluation mechanism based on 'question essence' and 'retrieval consistency'. It significantly outperforms baselines on Chinese legal benchmarks (SocialLawQA, LawBench) and shows strong cross-jurisdictional generalization on English datasets (LexGLUE, CaseHold). This method addresses a practical challenge in legal AI: balancing answer quality and efficiency for questions of varying complexity. It offers a novel adaptive routing approach that could improve legal consultation systems and inspire similar complexity-aware designs in other high-risk domains. On Chinese datasets, CoAL-RAG improves BLEU score by 42.5% and ROUGE-L reaches 3.6 times that of knowledge graph-based methods. On English benchmarks, it maintains competitive accuracy, achieving an optimal balance between generation quality, deep logical reasoning, and system efficiency across different legal systems.

**Background**: Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) is a technique that enables large language models to retrieve and incorporate new information from external data sources. In legal AI, questions range from simple to complex, and a single retrieval strategy often fails to handle this variability efficiently. CoAL-RAG leverages the discrepancy between semantic and keyword retrieval to infer question complexity, enabling adaptive strategy selection.

## [DMT-Dens: Density-Preserving Manifold Visualization for Biological Data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.17571) ⭐️ 7.0/10

DMT-Dens is a new parametric manifold-visualization method that uses a latent-token Transformer encoder to preserve sampling density in 2D embeddings of high-dimensional biological data. It optimizes a loss based on Pearson correlation between k-nearest-neighbor log-radius estimates in the input and embedding spaces. This method addresses a known limitation of existing embedding techniques that distort apparent sampling density, which can mislead interpretation of rare or transitional cell states. It could improve single-cell data analysis and visualization, benefiting researchers in genomics and computational biology. The model integrates rank-based manifold alignment with hard-pair aggregation. Benchmark evaluations show strong density preservation, especially on biological datasets, while retaining competitive label separability. Source code is available on GitHub.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 19, 04:00

**Background**: Low-dimensional embeddings like t-SNE and UMAP are widely used to explore cell-state heterogeneity in single-cell data. However, these methods often distort the apparent density of points, making dense and sparse regions look similar, which complicates interpretation of rare or continuous cell populations. DMT-Dens aims to preserve density by explicitly optimizing a density-related loss.

The paper introduces tinyDSM, a framework that enables cm-sized millirobots to autonomously develop skills using intrinsic motivation and fitness-based assessment, with minimal hard-wired knowledge. In experiments, a 36 cm³ millirobot with a Raspberry Pi Pico RP2040 microcontroller learned from basic motor skills to complex geometric patterns within 15 minutes. This work addresses the challenge of open-ended skill development in extremely resource-constrained robots, which is crucial for deploying autonomous systems in real-world environments where external rewards are sparse. It demonstrates that complex behaviors can emerge from minimal prior knowledge, potentially impacting fields like medical robotics and micro-manufacturing. The framework integrates a hierarchical knowledge graph and kinematic reasoners to model and evaluate motion skills, and it uses intrinsic motivation to drive exploration. The physical experiments used a 36 cm³ millirobot with a Raspberry Pi Pico RP2040, and the system fit all features except the camera in 9 kB of memory; simulation-based analysis complemented the physical tests.

A new survey paper (arXiv:2608.17605) reviews multi-turn conversational AI across text-only dialogue, AudioLLMs, multimodal and omni-modal systems, and tool-augmented agents. It organizes the literature around datasets, models, training strategies, and evaluation, highlighting that multimodal perception has outpaced sustained interaction capabilities. This survey provides a timely and comprehensive resource for researchers and developers working on conversational AI, especially as systems move toward multimodal and omni-modal interaction. It identifies key open challenges—such as persistent memory, cross-turn grounding, and cultural alignment—that must be addressed to build more capable and coherent dialogue systems. The survey covers datasets and benchmarks, modeling paradigms, training strategies, and evaluation setups for multi-turn dialogue. It notes that despite advances in perceiving, speaking, and acting across modalities, current systems still struggle with persistent memory, cross-turn grounding, full-duplex interaction, robust evaluation, and cultural alignment.

## [SC2R: Semantics-Constrained Counterfactual Recourse for Education](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.17618) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces SC2R, a semantics-constrained counterfactual recourse framework for educational decision support, which combines a calibrated predictive model, integer-programming-based recourse generation, an RDF vocabulary for intervention plans, and SHACL validation to enforce constraints. It is evaluated on the OULAD dataset, showing that semantic validation reveals infeasible plans that optimization-only settings would accept. This work addresses a practical gap in learning analytics by ensuring that intervention recommendations are not only model-valid but also semantically feasible and machine-checkable, making counterfactual recourse more operationally meaningful in education. It could impact how educational institutions plan interventions for at-risk students, improving the actionability of predictive models. The framework uses integer programming over discrete action variables to generate compact intervention plans, and SHACL validation enforces timing, budget, immutability, and availability constraints. The evaluation uses snapshots constructed relative to each assessment at two decision horizons on the OULAD dataset, demonstrating strong predictive performance and scalable plan generation.

This paper introduces a Deep Q-Network (DQN) framework that iteratively refines initial grasp candidates using keypoint-based object representations from 2D overhead images. In experiments on 300 Dex-Net objects with a UR5 manipulator, the framework achieved a 100% success rate on objects previously deemed ungraspable by geometrical methods, and sim-to-real transfer was validated on a Delta parallel robot. It has practical implications for robotic grasping in unstructured environments, potentially improving the reliability of automated systems in industries like logistics and manufacturing. The framework uses keypoint-based object representations, which encode task-specific knowledge as relationships between keypoint skeletons and suitable grasp points, preserving robustness to intra-class variations. The sim-to-real transfer was validated through physical experiments on a Delta parallel robot, where a refined grasp successfully manipulated a previously ungraspable object.

The paper introduces GADR, a multi-agent, self-correcting workflow that automatically extracts architectural decisions from raw meeting transcriptions and generates Nygard-formatted ADR drafts. A feasibility study with five real project transcripts, expert review by four senior architects, and evaluation by fifteen students shows it outperforms zero-shot and few-shot baselines in stability and structural adherence. The workflow includes a feasibility study with five real project meeting transcripts, expert review by four senior architects, and evaluation by fifteen students.
