## [Dijkstra as Exact Oracle for Stochastic Shortest Path Navigation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.17703) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper proves that Dijkstra's algorithm can serve as an exact oracle for stochastic shortest path problems under a condition weaker than causality, and introduces an online learning algorithm DORA with provable guarantees. This result challenges the conventional belief that Dijkstra is inexact for stochastic transitions, potentially enabling faster and more efficient planning in robotics and navigation. DORA's performance matches optimistic value iteration with significantly less computation, which could improve real-time decision-making in dynamic environments. DORA calls a shortest path oracle a fixed number of times per episode, never estimates a transition kernel, and adds a logarithmic survival weight for contact constraints. In experiments, it performed 4.5 to 19.3 times less planner work than optimistic value iteration and reduced contacts by a factor of seventeen.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 19, 04:00

**Background**: The stochastic shortest path problem generalizes the classic shortest path problem where edge traversal costs are probabilistic. Dijkstra's algorithm is a fast, exact method for deterministic graphs, but its applicability to stochastic settings has been limited. This work relaxes the causality condition, broadening the scope of exact planning.

This paper evaluates using large language models (LLMs) to translate technical credit risk model explanations into stakeholder-friendly narratives, testing three pipelines (XGBoost+SHAP, GNN+GNNExplainer, and a bimodal approach) on Freddie Mac data with three LLM configurations (Gemma 3 4B, DeepSeek R1 70B, and Gemini 2.5). This research addresses a critical gap in high-stakes credit decisioning where model outputs must be both accurate and explainable for compliance. By showing that LLMs can generate stakeholder-appropriate narratives, it could improve transparency and fairness in credit approvals, affecting both financial institutions and consumers. The study found that the pipeline (evidence representation) accounts for higher variance in evidence-grounding scores than the language model, indicating the binding constraint is the evidence representation. Additionally, narratives reliably name influential factors but are less reliable in stating the direction of influence, and professionals apply stricter evidentiary standards than non-professionals.

## [Learnware-Based CSI Feedback Repository Boosts 6G Performance](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.17760) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a Learnware-based model repository for CSI feedback in 6G, where scene-specific small models are stored with specifications and retrieved via statistical fingerprints. The approach achieves over 90% selection accuracy and improves performance by 18.8% (LOS) and 57.7% (NLOS) over a General Model, while reducing fine-tuning needs by up to 1000 samples and 100 epochs. This work addresses the trade-off between model generalization and scenario-specific performance in CSI feedback, a critical challenge for 6G. By enabling efficient model reuse and privacy-preserving deployment, it could significantly reduce computational costs and accelerate the adoption of AI-driven CSI feedback in real-world base stations. The repository uses a Learnware framework where each model has a specification with semantic (network architecture) and statistical (codebook-fingerprint embeddings) parts. A data-driven search strategy matches codebook fingerprints to model performance, achieving over 90% selection accuracy, and simulations show significant gains with reduced fine-tuning requirements.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 19, 04:00

**Background**: CSI feedback is essential for 6G to achieve high capacity and spectral efficiency, but deep learning solutions face a trade-off between generalization and scenario-specific performance. The Learnware paradigm, developed by the LAMDA group, provides a framework for submitting, organizing, and reusing models with specifications, enabling efficient model deployment. This paper applies Learnware to CSI feedback, allowing base stations to retrieve pre-trained models without transmitting raw data.

## [Synthetic Data Training for Drone Detection in Thermal Imagery](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.17799) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper proposes a synthetic-first training strategy for drone detection in MWIR/LWIR imagery, showing that fine-tuning on small amounts of real data significantly reduces domain gaps. It also finds that dataset alignment and semantic feature alignment are more critical than model scale. This work addresses the practical challenge of scarce annotated thermal data for drone detection, offering a cost-effective approach that combines synthetic and real data. The insights on dataset alignment can guide future domain adaptation efforts in computer vision, potentially improving deployment reliability in security and surveillance applications. The study uses medium- and long-wave infrared (MWIR/LWIR) imagery, which suffers from reduced texture, sensor noise, and weak thermal contrast. Experiments show that even small amounts of real IR data substantially reduce domain gaps, and semantic alignment in feature space is the strongest predictor of performance, while radiometric properties like entropy and dynamic range also contribute.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 19, 04:00

**Background**: Ground-to-air (G2A) drone detection in thermal imagery is challenging due to limited annotated data and difficult imaging conditions. Synthetic data generation offers a way to create large labeled datasets, but models trained solely on synthetic data often fail to generalize to real-world domains. Domain adaptation techniques aim to bridge this gap by aligning source and target distributions. This paper explores a synthetic-first approach with fine-tuning on real data to improve performance.

## [AdaLens: Storyline-Based Oversight for Long-Running Agentic Data Analysis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.17834) ⭐️ 7.0/10

AdaLens is an interactive system that introduces a storyline-based visualization to monitor and steer long-running agentic data analysis workflows. It combines analytical plans, execution progress, intermediate findings, and data-column involvement into a unified representation, with steering interactions for directional guidance and execution control. As AI agents become more autonomous in data analysis, conventional interfaces fail to provide adequate observability and steerability. AdaLens addresses this gap, potentially improving human oversight and trust in long-running agentic systems, which is crucial for real-world adoption. AdaLens supports multi-granularity inspection and data-grounded analytical lineage, with steering at both intention and execution levels. It was evaluated through two case studies and a user study, focusing on monitoring and steering long-running agentic data analysis.

## [Auditing the Decode-Generate-Steer Gap in Frozen LLMs for Geometric Constraints](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.17843) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper audits the decode-generate-steer gap in frozen LLMs for geometric constraints, finding that pretraining improves local relation decoding but not sketch-level status, which is already decodable from random initialization. This research provides a controlled testbed to distinguish failures to encode geometric structure from failures to express or control encoded information, which could inform future interpretability research and improve our understanding of LLM capabilities. The study probes hidden states of six frozen decoder-only LLMs, examining four properties: linear decodability, forced-choice generation, activation-level influence, and behavioral steerability. Results show that decodable information is not always actionable, as generation often fails to express it and steering does not reliably control outputs.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 19, 04:00

**Background**: Large language models (LLMs) have shown strong performance on structured reasoning tasks, but what they encode internally and whether that informs behavior remains unclear. Geometric reasoning, specifically parametric CAD constraints, serves as a controlled testbed to separate local pairwise relations from sketch-level constraint status. Probing hidden states involves training lightweight classifiers to detect specific information from internal representations, while steerability refers to the ability to control model outputs through interventions.

## [BEAR-Bench: Bilingual Benchmark for Multimodal Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.17895) ⭐️ 7.0/10

BEAR-Bench is a new bilingual (English/Russian) benchmark with 1,000 human-annotated questions for evaluating multimodal large language models on text-rich business and scientific documents. The study evaluates 16 proprietary and open-weight MLLMs, including Gemini 3.1 Pro and Qwen3.5-397B, revealing significant headroom even for the strongest systems. This benchmark addresses a critical gap in evaluating MLLMs on text-dense professional documents, which are underrepresented in existing benchmarks that are largely English- or Chinese-centric. By introducing a Russian component, it promotes multilingual evaluation and highlights the need for better reasoning capabilities in real-world document understanding. BEAR-Bench is self-contained and requires context-grounded, multi-step reasoning over text-rich documents, avoiding reliance on external domain knowledge. The paper also uses model outputs to compare hallucination detection methods, evaluating not only failure rates but also the reliability of detecting those failures.

It finds that DETR models show promising results, but all models struggle with small objects in air-to-ground scenarios. This study demonstrates the feasibility of using publicly available civilian models for military ATD/R, addressing the scarcity of military datasets. Performance is measured using mAP@0.5 and mAP@0.5:0.95, and the study finds that fine-tuning on VisDrone improves A2G performance and slightly improves small object detection, but in-domain training remains crucial.

## [Graph-Based Difficulty Estimator Boosts RLVR Rollout Efficiency](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.17941) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a plug-and-play graph-based online difficulty estimator for RLVR that shares rollout feedback across related samples and continuously updates difficulty estimates, addressing cold start and staleness without dedicated probing. This work improves the efficiency of rollout allocation in RLVR for large language models, potentially reducing training costs and improving reasoning performance. It offers a practical solution that can be integrated into existing schedulers, benefiting the AI/ML community focused on efficient RL training. The method constructs a difficulty-aware sample graph based on semantic and reasoning similarities, uses a Potts prior to encourage neighboring samples to share latent difficulty states, and employs a state-level Beta-Binomial model with online mean-field variational inference to update estimates. Experiments across multiple base models, RL schedulers, and benchmarks demonstrate better performance.

**Background**: Reinforcement Learning with Verifiable Rewards (RLVR) enhances reasoning in large language models but requires costly rollout exploration. Existing adaptive schedulers often rely on curriculum-based selection or non-uniform allocation based on difficulty estimates, but obtaining reliable online estimates is challenging due to cold start and staleness. This paper addresses these issues by leveraging graph structure to share feedback among related samples.

## [SIGMA: SHAP-Guided Implicit Trajectory for Metadata-Free AutoFE](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.17948) ⭐️ 7.0/10

SIGMA introduces a SHAP-guided implicit-trajectory generation method for metadata-free LLM-based automated feature engineering, achieving constant-context optimization without semantic metadata. The method reduces the duplicate ratio of generated features from 37.2% to 6.8% and matches state-of-the-art performance with only 5.4 features on average. This work addresses two critical limitations in LLM-based AutoFE: the unavailability of semantic metadata in many real-world settings and the context window overflow caused by trajectory accumulation. By enabling constant-context optimization without metadata, SIGMA enhances the scalability and applicability of LLM-driven feature engineering, potentially benefiting a wide range of machine learning practitioners. SIGMA uses SHAP values to provide task-aware signals for guiding group feature generation instead of semantic information. It adopts an EXposed-feature Implicit Trajectory (EXIT) approach, where exposed features in the prompt implicitly represent the trajectory, keeping prompt length nearly constant.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 19, 04:00

**Background**: Automated Feature Engineering (AutoFE) aims to automatically create and select features to improve machine learning model performance. Recent approaches use Large Language Models (LLMs) with semantic descriptions and trajectory-based prompting, but they face challenges when semantic metadata is unavailable and when long trajectories exceed context windows. SHAP (SHapley Additive exPlanations) values are a game-theoretic method to explain model predictions by assigning importance to each feature, which SIGMA leverages as a substitute for semantic information.

## [LoRD: Post-Hoc Calibration for Reliable Log Anomaly Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.17965) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces LoRD, a lightweight post-hoc calibration framework that improves confidence estimates in language model-based log anomaly detectors. It learns prediction-route-specific reliability models and selectively recalibrates high-risk predictions to suppress overconfident errors. This addresses a critical reliability gap in log anomaly detection, where models often assign excessive confidence to incorrect predictions, especially for anomalous logs under class imbalance. By improving calibration, LoRD enhances the trustworthiness of monitoring systems, which is vital for large-scale computing infrastructure. LoRD uses latent representations of correctly classified validation samples to estimate prediction reliability via route-wise reconstruction distances. Experiments on four large-scale log benchmark datasets and multiple language model-based detectors show consistent improvements in confidence reliability and substantial reduction in overconfident anomaly-related errors without sacrificing detection performance.

This paper introduces a source-free domain adaptation framework for ultrasound tongue segmentation that adapts from only five labeled source images to unlabeled target domains using pseudo-label refinement and synthetic data generation. This work addresses the critical challenge of domain shift in medical imaging, where annotated data is scarce and expensive. By enabling adaptation without access to source data, it could significantly improve the generalization of ultrasound tongue segmentation models across different datasets and devices, benefiting clinical applications. The framework uses a lightweight UltraUNet backbone, iteratively refines pseudo-labels, filters unreliable masks with a contour-based quality-control module, and generates target-style synthetic image-mask pairs via a segmentation-guided conditional GAN. It was evaluated on 12 source-target transfer pairs across eight ultrasound tongue imaging datasets, showing improvements over baselines including supervised ones.

## [Traceable Trust framework for trustworthy AI in bioscience](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.17997) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces 'Traceable Trust', a novel framework for assessing and designing AI systems in bioscience, focusing on the output-to-action boundary where AI outputs guide laboratory actions. It defines six key questions—evidence, capability, agency, threshold, override, and outcomes—to document trust in AI-driven decisions. As AI becomes integral to bioscience research, ensuring trustworthy decisions when AI outputs guide laboratory actions is critical. This framework provides a structured, reviewable process that could enhance reproducibility and accountability in AI-driven scientific work, impacting researchers, labs, and regulatory bodies. The framework is illustrated through three case studies spanning ecosystem resources, project design, and laboratory action. It emphasizes proportionate assessment, meaning the depth of scrutiny should match the risk and context of the AI application.

## [LLM Reward Shaping Preserves Optimal Policy in Hybrid RL](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.18008) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper formalizes a hybrid LLM-planner and RL-controller architecture as a Goal-Augmented Markov Decision Process and proves that using LLM per-state progress scores as a bounded potential function preserves the optimal policy set even when the scores are inaccurate. The result is verified numerically on a small MDP with four potential configurations, including an adversarial one scaled to twenty times the base reward. This provides a theoretical foundation for using LLM feedback as reward shaping in reinforcement learning, which is increasingly common in hybrid LLM-RL systems. The guarantee that optimal policies are preserved even with inaccurate LLM scores is stronger than general LLM-as-reward approaches, potentially enabling more reliable integration of LLMs into RL agents. The paper introduces a Goal-Augmented Markov Decision Process (GA-MDP) to model the hybrid architecture. The key theoretical result is that a bounded potential function based on LLM progress scores preserves the optimal policy set, even under adversarial scaling of the shaping reward to twenty times the base reward magnitude.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 19, 04:00

**Background**: Reward shaping is a technique in reinforcement learning to provide additional reward signals to guide learning, especially in sparse-reward environments. Potential-based reward shaping (PBRS), introduced by Ng et al. (1999), guarantees that the optimal policy remains unchanged when the shaping reward is the difference of a potential function. This paper extends PBRS to LLM-derived potential functions, providing a theoretical basis for using LLM feedback in RL.

## [Magnitude-Informed Deep Learning Improves Dynamic MRI Reconstruction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.18036) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces C+Mag, a magnitude-informed physics-driven deep learning method for accelerated steady-state dynamic MRI reconstruction, which leverages auxiliary k-space magnitude information. It employs an ADMM-based unrolling framework with a novel magnitude-aware data-fidelity formulation, demonstrating improved artifact suppression, sharper anatomical recovery, and better phase preservation compared to conventional PD-DL methods. This work addresses an underexplored area in MRI reconstruction by showing that magnitude-only measurements can complement complex measurements, potentially improving diagnostic image quality in dynamic imaging. It offers a practical approach that could enhance clinical MRI workflows without requiring additional scan time, benefiting both researchers and clinicians. The method introduces quadratically smoothed optimization and momentum-based updates to handle the non-differentiability and non-convexity of magnitude constraints. Experiments were conducted on retrospectively undersampled cine MRI and phase-contrast flow MRI datasets, as well as prospectively undersampled real-time cine MRI acquisitions, with blinded expert reader evaluations supporting the improvements.

**Background**: MRI reconstruction from undersampled k-space data is an ill-posed inverse problem, often addressed with physics-driven deep learning (PD-DL) methods that unroll iterative optimization algorithms. Traditional methods use complex-valued measurements, but this work explores the use of magnitude-only measurements, which have been shown in sparse phase retrieval to provide complementary information. The proposed method builds on the observation that k-space magnitudes are strongly consistent across time-frames in dynamic MRI, enabling the use of auxiliary magnitude information without extra scan time.

The paper introduces a capability-driven data infrastructure with three specialized data engines and a multi-stage curriculum scheduler, curating 440M T2I images, 120M editing pairs, and 27M image-entity pairs, and trains 3B and 6B multimodal diffusion models from scratch. This work addresses a key challenge in organizing heterogeneous supervision for generalist image generation, potentially improving model versatility and transfer across capabilities. It could influence future data curation and training strategies in the field. The infrastructure couples capability-specific supervision construction with capability-aligned curriculum scheduling, and includes capability-aware evaluation for targeted retrieval and gap-aware resampling. The models are evaluated on CPI-Bench and qualitative scenarios.

The paper proposes a capability-driven approach to align supervision with generative capabilities.

## [LLMs Show Generalizable Rule-Application Competence](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.00992) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new preprint (arXiv:2503.00992) tests 13 LLMs across five experiments, finding that their rule-application judgments closely track human judgments, even for stimuli created after the models' training cut-offs. The study also reveals that responses to time-pressure instructions are model-specific, and that smaller models replicate human patterns more closely. This research challenges the notion that LLM performance merely reflects memorization, suggesting a deeper, generalizable semantic competence in rule application. It has implications for how we evaluate LLM capabilities and for deploying LLMs in tasks requiring nuanced rule-following, such as legal or ethical reasoning. The study includes five experiments: 1A compares LLM judgments with human data on published and post-cutoff stimuli; 2A/2B test time-pressure instructions; 3 varies reasoning effort; 1B/2B vary system prompt wording and numerical scale anchors. Results show that increasing reasoning effort produced no detectable change for most models, though a purposivist trend appeared in higher-effort settings for GPT-oss and Claude Sonnet 5. Response variance remained lower for LLMs than humans despite temperature calibration.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 19, 04:00

**Background**: Large language models (LLMs) have matched or exceeded human performance on many tasks, but critics argue this may be due to memorization rather than true understanding. This study uses psychological methods to probe conceptual mastery, specifically in applying rules where text and purpose may conflict. The researchers introduced a novel procedure to match the diversity of LLM-generated thought to human samples, and used post-training-cutoff stimuli to rule out memorization.

## [Dynamic Shields Adapt to Changing Safety Specifications](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.22104) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces dynamic shields for parametric safety specifications, which can adapt at runtime to changing safety requirements without recomputation from scratch. The approach was tested on a robot navigation problem, showing up to 5 times faster online adaptation compared to brute-force recomputation. This work addresses a critical limitation of traditional static shields, which require costly recomputation when safety specifications change, potentially causing fatal delays in safety-critical AI systems. Dynamic shields enable real-time adaptation, improving the practicality and reliability of runtime safety enforcement for autonomous systems. The dynamic adaptation procedure leverages known features of standard safety shields, such as maximal permissiveness, to achieve a simple and fast algorithm. In experiments, offline design took a few minutes, while online adaptation took between a fraction of a second and a few seconds per step, significantly outperforming brute-force recomputation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 19, 04:00

**Background**: Shielding is a runtime safety enforcement technique for AI-controlled systems, where a shield monitors and intervenes in the controller's actions to prevent unsafe behavior. Traditional shields are designed statically for specific safety requirements, so any change in requirements necessitates recomputation. Parametric safety specifications succinctly represent all possible safety specifications that may be encountered, enabling dynamic adaptation.

## [Causal Prompt Engineering with Expert Mental Models Reduces LLM Hallucination](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.10818) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a causal prompt engineering framework that encodes domain experts' tacit decision logic as an Expert Mental Model (EMM) using monotone Boolean and k-valued function theory, reducing LLM hallucinations caused by unrecorded knowledge. It formalizes three prerequisite processes—factor formulation, factor monotonization, and monotonicity-preserving hierarchical structuring—enabling a tractable four-step EMM construction algorithm. This work addresses a fundamental limitation of LLMs: hallucination due to knowledge that exists nowhere in writing, which RAG and knowledge-graph methods cannot overcome. By providing a principled way to supply tacit expert knowledge, it could improve decision-making in high-stakes domains like grant evaluation, cybersecurity, and clinical diagnosis, and enables formal measurement of hallucination when expert knowledge is entirely tacit. The framework reduces elicitation from millions of combinations to a tractable, formally bounded set manageable by humans. Analysis shows conditional error rates on true positives can approach 100% for plausible LLM-expert mismatches that are invisible to prior evaluation methods. The approach is validated across grant proposal evaluation, cybersecurity design, and clinical diagnosis.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 19, 04:00

**Background**: Large language models (LLMs) often hallucinate when asked about knowledge that is not recorded in their training data or retrievable from external sources. Retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) and knowledge graphs can only supply information that has been explicitly documented, leaving a structural gap for tacit expert knowledge. Monotone Boolean and k-valued functions are mathematical tools used to model decision logic that is consistent and monotonic, meaning adding more positive evidence does not reverse a decision. Causal prompt engineering aims to incorporate cause-and-effect reasoning into prompts to improve LLM reliability.

Chronos, an AI co-historian, has been introduced to support historians by allowing them to create and customize research workflows through natural language interaction. Its first extension, Chronos-Extract, automates targeted information extraction from image scans of historical documents, achieving high task-accuracy across three centuries of diverse sources. This addresses the limited adoption of AI in historical research by providing a specialized, non-technical, and highly customizable tool. It could significantly accelerate historical analysis and foster broader AI integration in digital humanities. Chronos is built on an AI agent framework and integrates with a VS Code extension, enabling document analysis and structured data extraction. It is openly available and supports sharing workflows as Chronos-Extensions, with benchmarks on three historical corpora showing high accuracy across languages, layouts, and typefaces.

## [MemJack: Memory-Augmented Multi-Agent Jailbreak Attacks on VLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.12616) ⭐️ 7.0/10

MemJack is a novel memory-augmented multi-agent framework that automates jailbreak attacks on vision-language models (VLMs) using natural, unmodified images. It also introduces MemJack-Bench, a large benchmark dataset of over 113,000 interactive multimodal jailbreak trajectories for safety evaluation. This work exposes a significant vulnerability in safety-aligned VLMs, showing that benign natural images can serve as transferable jailbreak anchors. It highlights an underexplored attack surface and provides a valuable benchmark for future safety research and defensive alignment. MemJack achieves a 71.48% attack success rate (ASR) against Qwen3-VL-Plus on the full COCO val2017 dataset, scaling to 90% with extended budgets. It outperforms representative multimodal jailbreak baselines in the same natural-image setting, requiring fewer mean rounds to success.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 19, 04:00

**Background**: Vision-language models (VLMs) combine visual perception with text generation, expanding the attack surface for safety-aligned systems. Existing multimodal jailbreak attacks often rely on crafted visual content or adversarial perturbations, but MemJack explores the potential of reusable visual anchors in benign natural images, using a multi-agent closed-loop pipeline with visual-anchor discovery, semantic camouflage, response evaluation, reflection-guided repair, and dynamic replanning.

## [PEC-CIR: Training-Free Zero-Shot Composed Image Retrieval via Multi-Stage Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.31222) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces PEC-CIR, a training-free framework for zero-shot composed image retrieval that structures query construction as a multi-stage reasoning pipeline with a Planner-Executor-Critic architecture, improving retrieval stability by explicitly evaluating candidate queries before retrieval. This work addresses a key limitation of single-pass generation in composed image retrieval, where semantic distortions and omissions often degrade accuracy. By introducing a training-free, multi-stage reasoning approach, it offers a practical improvement that can be applied to existing frozen vision-language models without additional training, benefiting the broader AI/ML community working on retrieval and multimodal reasoning. PEC-CIR operates through a Planner that extracts explicit constraints, an Executor that generates multiple candidate target descriptions, and a Critic that evaluates these candidates based on constraint compliance. This staged inference process reduces the propagation of generative errors, improving retrieval stability in zero-shot settings.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 19, 04:00

**Background**: Composed image retrieval (CIR) is a task where users search for images using a reference image and a textual modification, such as 'make the shirt blue.' Traditional methods often require fine-tuning on labeled triplets, but zero-shot approaches aim to work without such training by leveraging pre-trained vision-language models like CLIP. Existing zero-shot methods typically generate a single textual query in one pass, which can lead to errors that propagate to retrieval. PEC-CIR reframes this as a multi-stage reasoning process to mitigate these issues.

## [FormalAnalyticGeo: Neural-Symbolic Framework for Generating Analytic Geometry Problems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.12982) ⭐️ 7.0/10

FormalAnalyticGeo introduces a scalable framework that automatically generates multimodal analytic geometry problems using a formal intermediate representation (CDL) and a Signed Distance Field (SDF) engine. It produces the AnalyticGeo7K dataset with over 7,000 verified problems, each containing aligned text, diagram, formal annotation, and ground truth. This framework addresses the scarcity of annotated analytic geometry samples for training Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs), which is a critical gap in math reasoning research. By automating data generation with high geometric precision, it can significantly accelerate the development of MLLMs' capabilities in analytic geometry and related domains. The framework employs four specialized LLM components: Generator, Formalizer, Measurer, and Quality Verifier, with structured feedback enabling automatic retry without human annotation. Experiments show a median ground-truth relative error of 0.70%, with 82.3% of answers within 5% of the exact symbolic solution.

Neural-symbolic AI combines neural networks with symbolic reasoning to improve reliability and precision, which is relevant here because the framework uses formal languages to bridge text and diagrams.

## [JUMP: Efficient Membership Inference for Diffusion Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.16207) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces JUMP (Joint Uncertainty-Guided Mask Probing), a novel method for membership inference attacks on fine-tuned discrete diffusion language models (dLLMs). JUMP selects low-reference-confidence positions, masks them jointly, and aggregates clipped target-reference reconstruction gaps, achieving higher ROC-AUC scores with fewer model forwards compared to the existing SAMA baseline. This work advances privacy auditing for diffusion-based language models, which are increasingly used in real-world applications. By improving the efficiency and accuracy of membership inference, it enables more effective detection of unauthorized data usage and contributes to the broader discussion on model privacy and compliance. JUMP requires only three model forwards per sample, compared to 32 for SAMA, while improving mean ROC-AUC from 0.819 to 0.902 on LLaDA-8B-Base and from 0.851 to 0.942 on Dream-v0-Base-7B across six MIMIR domains. The method leverages the parallel prediction capability of dLLMs to jointly mask multiple positions, avoiding the dilution of informative signals.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 19, 04:00

**Background**: Membership inference attacks (MIAs) aim to determine whether a specific data record was part of a model's training set, posing privacy risks. Discrete diffusion language models (dLLMs) generate text through iterative denoising from corrupted states, allowing parallel predictions for multiple masked positions. The pre-fine-tuning checkpoint serves as a reference to identify training data, as fine-tuned models often exhibit different reconstruction behavior on their training samples.

## [G-ReAct: Graph-Guided Deep Search Boosts LLM Multi-Hop Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.01324) ⭐️ 7.0/10

G-ReAct is a new reasoning framework that organizes deep search as state evolution over a fixed-topology query graph, addressing context forgetting and search drift. With only 1.9K generated trajectories for fine-tuning, Qwen3-30B-A3B-Thinking-2507 achieves 52.6% accuracy on BrowseComp-ZH and 79.0% on XBench, outperforming larger-scale trained open-source methods. This work addresses a critical limitation in LLM deep search—the loss of intermediate state and constraints during long-horizon multi-hop reasoning—which is essential for complex open-domain tasks. By providing both training and inference-time benefits, G-ReAct could improve the reliability and efficiency of LLM-based search and reasoning systems, impacting applications like question answering and research assistants. G-ReAct uses a dual-layer design with structure invariance and state evolution, where the graph state explicitly tracks search progress and guides decisions. It supports both supervised fine-tuning (generating high-quality trajectories) and inference-time guidance without additional fine-tuning, and the authors plan to release all code and model weights.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 19, 04:00

**Background**: Deep search in LLMs typically relies on linear sequential reasoning, which struggles to preserve intermediate states and constraints over long multi-hop searches, leading to context forgetting and search drift. G-ReAct introduces a graph-structured approach where the reasoning process is represented as a fixed-topology query graph, and the state evolves over this graph to guide the search. This is part of a broader trend in LLM reasoning that moves beyond chain-of-thought to more structured and tool-augmented methods.

The paper introduces VDGR-RAG, an agentic GraphRAG system that integrates vector retrieval, directory-driven reasoning, graph traversal, and iterative reflection in a unified framework for enterprise knowledge QA. It constructs a Hierarchical Heterogeneous Knowledge Graph (H²KG) and employs atomic tools for knowledge retrieval, demonstrating significant improvements over RAG baselines in wireless domain experiments. This work addresses a practical challenge in enterprise knowledge QA by holistically combining multiple retrieval strengths, which existing RAG approaches often overlook. It could improve accuracy and reasoning capabilities in complex domains like telecommunications, benefiting industries that rely on hierarchical product documentation. The system includes four key tools: a directory-enhanced routing tool using table-of-contents (TOC) structures, a multi-route retrieval tool combining vector, TOC-based agentic, and graph search, a directory backtracking tool to correct localization biases, and a dynamic reflection tool for iterative planning. Experiments were conducted on enterprise product documents across four wireless domains, showing significant gains in retrieval recall and QA accuracy.

## [Rationale-Guided Learning Boosts Multimodal Emotion Recognition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10448) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces Rationale-Guided Learning (RGL), a novel framework that reframes multimodal emotion recognition in conversation (MERC) as a cognitively-inspired reasoning task, decomposing emotional reasoning into intuitive, contextual, and integrative facets based on dual-process theory. RGL leverages a multimodal large language model (MLLM) offline to generate structured rationales that guide model training, achieving state-of-the-art performance on IEMOCAP and MELD benchmarks without any MLLM overhead at inference. This work addresses a fundamental limitation in existing MERC approaches, which treat emotion recognition as direct input-output mapping and overlook human causal reasoning. By introducing a reasoning-based framework that is both effective and efficient (no inference overhead), RGL could influence future research in affective computing and multimodal learning, offering a new paradigm for incorporating cognitive theories into model design. RGL decomposes emotional reasoning into three facets: Intuitive (System 1), Contextual (System 2), and Integrative (synthesis of both). The framework uses an MLLM offline to generate structured rationales, which are encoded as memories to align internal representations with human-like reasoning patterns; the final model operates without MLLM at inference time. Experiments show state-of-the-art results on IEMOCAP and MELD, and the model's internal features can retrieve semantically correct rationales for unseen test samples, validating its reasoning capabilities.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 19, 04:00

**Background**: Multimodal emotion recognition in conversation (MERC) uses language, visual, and audio cues to understand human emotions in dialogues. Dual-process theory, popularized by Daniel Kahneman and Amos Tversky, posits that human cognition operates through two systems: an intuitive, fast System 1 and an analytical, slow System 2. Multimodal large language models (MLLMs) are AI systems that can process and reason across multiple modalities such as text, images, and audio. This paper applies dual-process theory to MERC, using an MLLM to generate rationales that guide model training, aiming to incorporate human-like reasoning into emotion recognition.

## [Distribird Automates Informative Prior Design for Bayesian Calibration](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11210) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Distribird is an agentic web application that automates the creation of informative prior distributions for Bayesian model calibration by mining scientific literature and fitting distributions via AIC model selection. It was evaluated on 24 parameters across 10 domains, matching a single-prompt LLM baseline in prior quality while providing traceable evidence and local execution. This tool addresses a common bottleneck in applied Bayesian modeling, where researchers often default to uniform priors due to the time and expertise required for prior elicitation. By automating the process, Distribird could make informative priors more accessible, improving the reliability of model calibration across scientific domains. The system uses a multi-agent pipeline that searches literature, extracts and weights reported values by domain relevance, and fits a distribution via AIC model selection. It includes a validity layer that declines out-of-scope requests, and all language-model calls run locally, ensuring no parameter descriptions are transmitted to third-party providers.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 19, 04:00

**Background**: Bayesian model calibration requires specifying prior distributions for model parameters, but constructing informative priors from scientific literature is time-consuming and requires both domain and statistical expertise. Distribird automates this by using an agentic web application that searches literature and fits distributions, falling back to uninformative priors when no literature is available. AIC (Akaike Information Criterion) is a statistical method used for model selection, balancing goodness of fit with model complexity.

## [LLM-Guided Graph Generation Enhances Local Search for Combinatorial Optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13333) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces an automatic pipeline that uses LLMs to generate weighted graphs from MiniZinc problem instances, guiding a structure-based local improvement (SLIM) framework for variable selection. The approach achieves a 39.6% average problem-weighted win rate against a one-shot Gurobi baseline, more than doubling the best single configuration (19.3%). This work demonstrates a novel way to automate variable selection in local search using LLM-generated problem-agnostic graphs, potentially improving solver performance across diverse combinatorial optimization problems. It could lead to more efficient and generalizable optimization tools, benefiting researchers and practitioners in operations research and AI. The pipeline is problem-agnostic and works for all MiniZinc-format problems. It was evaluated on 20 MiniZinc competition problems, and a post-hoc configuration and feature ablation indicate a headroom of up to 44.0%, showing potential for further improvement.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 19, 04:00

**Background**: Large neighborhood search (LNS) is a metaheuristic for combinatorial optimization that iteratively modifies large portions of a solution. MiniZinc is a high-level constraint modeling language that can be translated to solver-specific formats like FlatZinc. The SLIM framework is a type of LNS that uses SAT-based local improvement for variable selection.

## [RETRACE: Resilience-Guided Craving Estimation from Wearable Physiology](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.14947) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces RETRACE, a resilience-guided trait-conditioned framework for subject-independent opioid craving estimation from wearable physiological signals. It achieves up to 7% absolute improvement over the strongest baseline in a leave-one-subject-out evaluation. This work addresses a critical challenge in health informatics: detecting opioid craving from wearable sensors to enable proactive interventions for opioid use disorder (OUD). By tackling subject variability and stress entanglement, RETRACE could improve personalized mental health monitoring and intervention timing. RETRACE uses a dual-encoder design: a frozen stress-pretrained encoder and a resilience-conditioned craving encoder, with feature-level gating and representation-level fusion. It leverages subject-level proxies like post-stress heart-rate recovery and autobiographical memory recall, avoiding the need for target-user craving labels or per-user retraining.

Psychological resilience influences stress regulation and craving vulnerability, but is not directly observable from short-term wearable windows; hence, reusable proxies are needed.

## [AdmitOR: Label-Free Admission Gate for LLM Optimization Modeling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.15565) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces AdmitOR, a label-free admission gate that uses calibrated behavioral evidence to decide whether to accept, abstain, or escalate candidate skills for LLM-based optimization modeling. It achieves an admission precision of 0.927, significantly outperforming baseline judges like majority vote (0.871) and execution success (0.726). This work addresses a critical bottleneck in experience learning for LLM-based optimization: the lack of labeled answers in real-world ticket streams. By enabling reliable label-free admission, AdmitOR can improve the safety and efficiency of autonomous optimization agents, reducing the risk of poisoning the skill library with incorrect knowledge. AdmitOR runs candidates from three model families, prompting strategies, and solver stacks on resampled instances, summarizes agreement via a cross-family clique, and applies a calibrated threshold. The preregistered false-discovery criterion held on calibration data but failed on the wild stream, with most failures traced to benchmark texts that do not faithfully encode their labeled instances.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 19, 04:00

**Background**: Experience-learning agents for optimization modeling improve by storing verified skills, but existing methods require known answers to admit new knowledge. Label-free alternatives like admitting every executable model or single-instance agreement are unreliable, often poisoning the library. AdmitOR uses calibrated behavioral evidence from multiple model families to make more robust admission decisions.

## [New Framework for Evaluating Lymphoma Segmentation DNNs from PET/CT](https://arxiv.org/abs/2311.09614) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This study proposes a clinically relevant evaluation framework for deep learning lymphoma segmentation from PET/CT images, incorporating out-of-distribution testing and comparison with expert observers. The framework was applied to four deep networks (ResUNet, SegResNet, DynUNet, and SwinUNETR) across 611 multi-institutional cases, introducing a new detection criterion based on metabolic characteristics. This framework addresses critical gaps in medical imaging AI, particularly the lack of out-of-distribution testing and comparison with human experts, which are essential for clinical adoption. By highlighting that networks perform similarly to experts on small and faint lesions, it underscores the need for improved algorithms and better understanding of task difficulty. The study evaluates networks using lesion-specific measures beyond standard Dice similarity, including detection criteria and prediction errors for clinical lesion measures. It demonstrates that networks perform better on large, intense lesions with higher metabolic activity, and that network errors closely resemble those made by experts, with small and faint lesions remaining challenging for both.

LSem2Vec is a new two-stage approach for source code embedding that combines an LLM to extract code semantics with a sentence embedding model to generate vectors, eliminating the need for task-specific training or fine-tuning. The paper demonstrates its effectiveness over five state-of-the-art unsupervised methods across three datasets. This approach reduces the cost and complexity of generating source code embeddings, making them more accessible for tasks like clone detection and clustering. It addresses the common issue of erroneous information in LLM outputs, potentially improving the robustness of code representation in software engineering. LSem2Vec uses an LLM to create summaries of source code fragments, which are then converted into embeddings via a sentence embedding model. The experiments cover multiple programming languages and various LLM and sentence embedding model combinations, showing superiority over five unsupervised baselines.

## [M3TR: Temporal Retrieval Enhanced Multi-Modal Micro-video Popularity Prediction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.15455) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces M3TR, a novel framework that integrates a Mamba-Hawkes Process (MHP) module for fine-grained temporal modeling of user feedback and a temporal-aware retrieval engine that considers both multi-modal content similarity and popularity trajectory similarity. Experiments on two real-world datasets show M3TR achieves state-of-the-art performance, outperforming previous methods by up to 19.3% in nMSE. This work addresses critical limitations in micro-video popularity prediction by capturing complex temporal engagement dynamics and incorporating temporal patterns into retrieval. It offers a significant improvement for applications like recommender systems and viral marketing, and introduces a novel integration of temporal event modeling with retrieval augmentation that could inspire future research. M3TR uses a Mamba-Hawkes Process to model user interactions as self-exciting events, capturing long-range dependencies. The temporal-aware retrieval engine combines multi-modal content (visual, audio, text) and popularity trajectories to retrieve historically relevant videos, augmenting the target video's features. The framework achieves up to 19.3% improvement in nMSE over previous methods on two real-world datasets.

The M3TR framework addresses these gaps by integrating temporal modeling with retrieval augmentation, a novel approach in this domain.

## [Bactrainus: Modular Selector-Reader Framework for Multi-hop QA](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.06286) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces Bactrainus, a modular selector-reader framework for multi-hop question answering on the HotpotQA distractor setting, which separates paragraph selection, supporting-sentence identification, and answer generation. The strongest configuration achieves 89.01 answer F1 and 79.70 joint F1, and ablations show that supplying full candidate context instead of gold supporting facts reduces answer token-overlap F1 by 17-21 points. This work addresses the evidence bottleneck in multi-hop QA, showing that even large language models remain sensitive to irrelevant context, and that explicit, auditable evidence interfaces can improve performance. It provides a practical framework for building more reliable and interpretable QA systems, which is important for applications requiring traceable reasoning. The framework uses Llama 3.1 8B Instruct and Llama 3.1 70B Instruct readers with parameter-efficient adaptation, and includes optional question decomposition and teacher-generated rationale supervision. The largest performance differences are associated with reader adaptation and sentence-level evidence control, while decomposition and rationale-supervision variants yield smaller, recipe-dependent changes.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 19, 04:00

**Background**: Multi-hop question answering (MHQA) requires a system to gather and integrate evidence from multiple documents to answer complex questions. The HotpotQA dataset is a popular benchmark for this task, featuring a distractor setting where each question is paired with two gold paragraphs and eight distractors. Traditional approaches often use a single model to generate answers directly, which can be sensitive to irrelevant context. Bactrainus instead separates the process into explicit stages, making the reasoning more auditable.

## [Fast MCTS with Information Gain Rewards Boosts LLM-Based KBQA](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.13428) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces Fast MCTS, a Monte Carlo Tree Search variant for LLM-based knowledge base question answering (KBQA) that replaces expensive terminal rollouts with an information gain (IG) reward computed via a question-conditioned PPL-ratio proxy. It consistently outperforms linear baselines and improves the accuracy-cost trade-off over Classic MCTS on four KBQA benchmarks. This work addresses two major challenges in applying MCTS to LLM reasoning: reward design and computational cost. By enabling efficient intermediate-state rewards without additional reward-model training, it makes MCTS-based reasoning more practical for KBQA and potentially other reasoning tasks, contributing to the broader trend of improving LLM reasoning with search algorithms. The IG reward is implemented as a question-conditioned PPL-ratio proxy over sanitized interaction histories, computed by forward passes of an open-source instruction LLM. The method requires no additional reward-model training and is evaluated on four KBQA benchmarks, showing consistent improvements over linear baselines and generally better accuracy-cost trade-offs than rollout-based Classic MCTS.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 19, 04:00

**Background**: Knowledge base question answering (KBQA) aims to answer natural language questions over structured knowledge bases. Large language models (LLMs) have been used for KBQA, but their reasoning can be improved with search algorithms like Monte Carlo Tree Search (MCTS), which explores multiple reasoning paths. However, applying MCTS to LLM-based KBQA is challenging because reward design is difficult and rollout-based search is computationally expensive. Information gain (IG) measures how much new information a step provides, and perplexity (PPL) is a metric indicating a model's confidence in its predictions, with lower PPL indicating higher confidence.

Researchers introduced TabularQGAN, a quantum generative adversarial network (QGAN) with flexible data encoding and a novel quantum circuit ansatz, designed specifically for synthesizing heterogeneous tabular data. It was benchmarked on the MIMIC-III healthcare and Adult Census datasets, showing competitive or leading performance against classical models like CTGAN, CopulaGAN, VAE-GMM, and an LLM-based approach (be-GReaT). The model was evaluated as a proof-of-concept on reduced feature subsets using a noiseless statevector simulator on classical hardware. The authors also introduced two custom-designed metrics to evaluate generalization capabilities, demonstrating the model's ability to generate useful and novel tabular samples.

## [ChannelFlow-Tools: Config-Driven Pipeline for ML-Ready 3D Channel Flow Datasets](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.15236) ⭐️ 7.0/10

ChannelFlow-Tools is an open-source, configuration-driven pipeline that generates machine-learning-ready datasets of 3D obstructed channel flows, integrating procedural geometry generation, SDF voxelization, and lattice-Boltzmann simulation. It was introduced in arXiv paper 2509.15236 and includes reproducibility and integrity audits. This pipeline addresses the need for controlled data generation in surrogate modeling for computational fluid dynamics, enabling researchers to regenerate, adapt, or extend datasets to match specific research requirements. It provides shared, auditable infrastructure for benchmarking geometry-aware CFD surrogates, potentially accelerating progress in data-driven fluid dynamics. The pipeline integrates six shape families for procedural obstacle geometry, signed-distance-field (SDF) voxelization, and lattice-Boltzmann simulation, packaging results into ML-ready tensors. It was validated through mesh-integrity audits, SDF validation, canonical sphere-flow benchmarks, and data-integrity audits, and demonstrated by training three surrogate models (3D U-Net, FNO, U-FNO) on 450 simulations with Reynolds numbers from ~1000 to 10,000.

## [Future-Back Threat Modeling: A Foresight-Driven Security Framework](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.16088) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces Future-Back Threat Modeling (FBTM), a predictive security framework that starts from envisioned future threat states and works backward to identify current vulnerabilities, assumptions, and blind spots. It aims to reveal known unknowns and unknown unknowns, including emerging, anticipated, and plausible tactics, techniques, and procedures (TTPs). FBTM addresses a critical gap in traditional threat modeling, which is often reactive and based on past incidents, while future threats like AI-driven attacks and supply chain compromises are increasingly significant. By providing a foresight-driven approach, it could help security leaders make proactive decisions to build more resilient defenses against emerging threats. The FBTM framework is grounded in five interrelated epistemic principles that differentiate it from traditional threat modeling, reframing how security foresight, validation, and governance interact across temporal and cognitive boundaries. However, the paper is theoretical and lacks empirical validation, as noted in the news item's score reason.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 19, 04:00

**Background**: Traditional threat modeling is a structured method for proactively identifying, assessing, and addressing risks before they become real-world failures or attacks. It often relies on known TTPs and past incident data, which can be insufficient against novel threats such as AI-enabled attacks and supply chain attacks, where adversaries continuously develop new exploits. FBTM aims to bridge this gap by working backward from future threat scenarios to current defenses.

This work addresses the dual threats of sophisticated deepfake synthesis attacks and control-plane poisoning in federated learning, which are critical for securing voice authentication systems deployed at the network edge. The framework fuses interpretable physics features modeling vocal tract dynamics with representations from a self-supervised learning module, processed via a Multi-Layer Perceptron backbone and a Bayesian ensemble for uncertainty estimates. ## [Cluster Aggregated GAN Improves Synthetic Appliance Data Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.22287) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces Cluster Aggregated GAN (CAG), a hybrid generative model that routes intermittent and continuous appliances to specialized branches, using clustering for intermittent appliances and an LSTM-based generator for continuous ones. Experiments on the UVIC smart plug dataset show CAG outperforms baseline methods in realism, diversity, and training stability. This work addresses a critical bottleneck in non-intrusive load monitoring (NILM) by improving the fidelity of synthetic appliance data, which is essential for training NILM algorithms and enabling privacy-preserving energy research. The specialized branch design offers a more scalable and interpretable approach compared to uniform GAN models, potentially accelerating progress in smart grid and energy efficiency applications. The CAG framework separates appliances into intermittent and continuous categories; intermittent appliances are clustered to allocate dedicated generators for common and rare modes, while continuous appliances use an LSTM-based generator with sequence compression for stability. The model consistently outperforms baselines on metrics measuring realism, diversity, and training stability, and integrating clustering as an active generative component improves interpretability and scalability.

## [Game-Theoretic Framework for Adversarial Epidemiological Data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.20134) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a novel framework that models the interaction between individuals and public health authorities as a signaling game, providing both a generative model for strategically corrupted behavioral data and a mechanism to recover reliable signals. The framework is analytically characterized for masking and vaccination scenarios, and validated through large-scale simulations and real-world data. This work addresses the critical and timely issue of adversarial data in public health, where individuals may misreport behaviors due to penalties, benefits, or distrust. By offering tools to design more robust epidemiological models, it has the potential to improve epidemic control and public health decision-making in the presence of strategic user behavior. The framework characterizes equilibrium outcomes as distinct regimes of data corruption and evaluates the tolerance for deception while maintaining epidemic control. Real-world validation shows that behavioral distortions often exhibit structured patterns rather than arbitrary noise, which is a key insight for modeling.

**Background**: Signaling games are a type of dynamic Bayesian game in game theory where one player (the sender) takes an action to convey information to another player (the receiver), and sending a false signal is costly. Epidemiological models increasingly rely on crowdsourced, self-reported data such as vaccination status and mask usage, which can be strategically misreported. This paper applies signaling game theory to model such adversarial data and recover reliable signals.

## [New Metrics for Evaluating RAG Fine-Tuning in Domain-Specific Document Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.23047) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces C-FEX, a claim-based evaluation pipeline, and Parametric Knowledge Precision (PKP), a new metric to assess parametric knowledge in RAG fine-tuning for requirements document generation in electronics engineering. It shows that fine-tuned 7B models can match or exceed a 72B baseline, and that standard metrics like ROUGE and BERTScore may mislead about fine-tuning gains. This work addresses a critical gap in evaluating factual accuracy for long technical text in RAG fine-tuning, which is often poorly captured by existing metrics. The proposed metrics could improve how RAG-SFT is assessed across specialized domains, potentially leading to more reliable and factually correct generated documents. The study adapts two 7B models under two different training data strategies, and introduces PKP which decomposes a prior metric into PKP × PR, separating the rate of parametric output from its quality. Empirically, fine-tuning does not reinforce correct parametric knowledge but suppresses hallucination, meaning models speak from their weights less often but more reliably.

## [AI Support Formats Vary in Effectiveness Across Tasks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.03237) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv preprint reports three controlled human-subject studies showing that the effectiveness of AI support formats—predictions, explanations, and probabilities—varies by task type. In visual reasoning (RAVEN matrices), explanations did not improve accuracy beyond predictions alone, while in logical reasoning (LSAT), LLM explanations outperformed other formats. This research challenges the assumption that explanations always improve human-AI team performance, highlighting the need for task-adaptive AI interfaces. It informs the design of decision support systems to foster calibrated reliance, potentially improving outcomes in high-stakes domains like medicine and law. The studies used RAVEN matrices for abstract visual reasoning and LSAT problems for deductive logical reasoning. Predicted probabilities showed the highest descriptive accuracy and error recovery in visual tasks, while a selective-automation policy provided a higher-performing benchmark. In logical tasks, LLM explanations yielded the highest accuracy and error recovery, outperforming expert-written explanations.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 19, 04:00

**Background**: Calibrated reliance refers to the appropriate level of trust humans place in AI, relying on it when correct and overriding when wrong. RAVEN matrices are non-verbal tests of abstract reasoning, while LSAT logical reasoning problems assess deductive logic. The study compares different AI support formats to see which best helps users calibrate their reliance.

This paper introduces a novel evaluation method that aligns sentences from 150 human-written novel summaries with the chapters they reference, then compares these with summaries generated by nine state-of-the-art LLMs. The findings reveal that LLMs tend to emphasize the ends of narratives, unlike human summarizers, indicating a divergence in conceptual engagement. This work addresses a critical gap in long-context LLM evaluation by focusing on conceptual engagement rather than mere factual recall. The findings have implications for improving LLM summarization capabilities and understanding their limitations in processing long-form texts, which is relevant for researchers and practitioners in NLP. The study uses 150 novels and aligns summary sentences to chapters, demonstrating the difficulty of this alignment task. It compares human summaries with those from nine LLMs, finding stylistic differences and a tendency for models to focus on text endings, which may explain degraded narrative comprehension.

## [SegWithU: Single-Pass Uncertainty via Perturbation Energy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.15271) ⭐️ 7.0/10

SegWithU introduces a post-hoc uncertainty head that attaches to a frozen segmentation backbone, modeling uncertainty as perturbation energy in a compact probe space using rank-1 posterior probes. It produces calibration- and ranking-oriented uncertainty maps, achieving state-of-the-art single-forward-pass results on ACDC, BraTS2024, and LiTS. This work addresses a critical need in clinical AI for reliable uncertainty estimation without the computational cost of repeated inference. By achieving strong performance with a single forward pass, SegWithU could enable practical deployment of risk-aware segmentation in real-time clinical workflows. SegWithU taps intermediate backbone features and uses perturbation energy in a probe space, producing two voxel-wise uncertainty maps: one for probability tempering and one for error detection. It reports AUROC/AURC of 0.9838/2.4885 on ACDC, 0.9946/0.2660 on BraTS2024, and 0.9925/0.8193 on LiTS, while preserving segmentation quality. Source code is available on GitHub.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 19, 04:00

**Background**: Medical image segmentation often relies on deep learning models, but these models can be overconfident in their predictions, which is dangerous in clinical settings. Uncertainty estimation methods typically require multiple forward passes (e.g., Monte Carlo dropout or ensembles), which is computationally expensive. SegWithU offers a post-hoc approach that adds a lightweight head to a frozen model, using perturbation energy to quantify uncertainty efficiently.

## [Self-Distillation Fine-Tuning Recovers LLM Performance Loss](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.15794) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces Self-Distillation Fine-Tuning (SDFT), a framework that restores LLM performance degraded by catastrophic forgetting, quantization, or pruning, and provides a theoretical explanation using Centered Kernel Alignment (CKA) to show that recovery correlates with manifold alignment. This work addresses critical challenges in LLM deployment—performance loss from compression and fine-tuning—offering a practical recovery method that could extend model lifespan and reduce the need for retraining. The theoretical analysis with CKA provides new insights into self-distillation mechanisms, potentially guiding future model optimization. The framework leverages a model's own predictions for fine-tuning, avoiding external teachers, and uses CKA to measure alignment between student and teacher activation trajectories. Experiments show a strong correlation between performance recovery and manifold alignment, substantiating the claim that self-distillation aligns the student's high-dimensional manifold with the teacher's optimal structure.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 19, 04:00

**Background**: Large language models (LLMs) often suffer from catastrophic forgetting during supervised fine-tuning (SFT), where they lose previously learned knowledge, and from performance degradation due to quantization and pruning. Self-distillation is a technique where a model learns from its own outputs, and Centered Kernel Alignment (CKA) is a metric for comparing neural network representations. This paper combines these concepts to propose a recovery framework.

## [FairNVT: Noise Injection Debiases Vision Transformers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.16780) ⭐️ 7.0/10

FairNVT introduces a lightweight debiasing framework for pretrained vision transformers that injects calibrated Gaussian noise into sensitive embeddings to reduce sensitive-attribute leakage, improving fairness metrics while preserving task performance. It was released as an arXiv preprint (2604.16780) and evaluated across three vision and language datasets. This work addresses the timely issue of fairness in AI by offering a practical, lightweight solution for pretrained models, which are widely used in real-world applications. By reducing sensitive-attribute leakage, FairNVT could help deploy more equitable vision systems without significant retraining costs. The framework learns task-relevant and sensitive embeddings via lightweight adapters, applies calibrated Gaussian noise to the sensitive embedding, and fuses it with the task representation. It also incorporates orthogonality constraints and fairness regularization to jointly reduce leakage, and demonstrates improvements in demographic parity difference and equalized odds across datasets.

**Background**: Vision transformers (ViTs) are powerful deep learning models for image tasks, but they can inadvertently encode sensitive attributes like race or gender from training data, leading to biased predictions. Fairness metrics such as demographic parity and equalized odds measure whether predictions are independent of sensitive attributes or have equal error rates across groups. Debiasing methods aim to remove or reduce such sensitive information from model representations while maintaining task accuracy.

## [SOD: Step-wise On-policy Distillation for Small Language Model Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.07725) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces SOD, a step-wise on-policy distillation framework that adaptively reweights distillation strength at each step based on step-level divergence to mitigate error cascades in tool-integrated reasoning for small language models. Experiments show SOD achieves up to 20.86% improvement over the second-best baseline, and a 0.6B student reaches 26.13% on AIME 2025. This work addresses a critical challenge in distilling tool-integrated reasoning to small language models, which is important for efficient deployment of capable AI agents. By improving the reliability of distillation, SOD could enable lightweight models to perform complex reasoning tasks that were previously only feasible with much larger models, impacting fields like math, science, and code generation. SOD adaptively reweights distillation strength based on step-level divergence, attenuating misleading teacher signals in high-divergence regions while preserving dense guidance in well-aligned states. The framework is evaluated on challenging math, science, and code benchmarks, and the code is available at https://github.com/YoungZ365/SOD.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 19, 04:00

**Background**: Tool-integrated reasoning (TIR) involves language models interacting with external tools like calculators or code interpreters to enhance reasoning. Distillation is a technique to transfer knowledge from a large teacher model to a smaller student model, and on-policy distillation uses student-generated trajectories for training. However, in TIR, errors can cascade across steps, making teacher supervision unreliable. SOD addresses this by adjusting distillation strength per step.

This paper introduces a dynamic framework that partitions and offloads neural network layers across heterogeneous edge-cloud environments, profiling the model at startup and periodically re-evaluating partitions based on network conditions. Evaluated on a physical testbed with Raspberry Pi, laptop, and desktop, it achieves 27.09-35.82% energy reduction and 6.34-22.92% latency reduction compared to static partitioning. The framework was tested on three CNNs: VGG16, AlexNet, and MobileNetV2, using a Raspberry Pi as edge, a laptop as fog, and a desktop as cloud. The adaptive approach periodically re-evaluates partitions to adapt to environmental changes, unlike static baselines.

<a id="item-258"></a>
## [SurgicalMamba: Dual-Path SSD with State Regramming for Online Surgical Phase Recognition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.14889) ⭐️ 7.0/10

SurgicalMamba introduces a dual-path structured state-space duality (SSD) model with state regramming and intensity-modulated stepping to improve online surgical phase recognition. It achieves state-of-the-art accuracy (94.6%/82.7% on Cholec80, 89.5%/68.9% on AutoLaparo) at 312.88 fps on a single GPU. This work addresses critical limitations in online surgical phase recognition, such as state overwriting and decay issues, which are common in long surgical videos. The proposed mechanisms could improve real-time surgical assistance systems and potentially generalize to other sequence modeling tasks. State regramming rotates the carried state at each chunk boundary using an input-conditioned orthogonal rotation (Cayley transform of a low-rank skew-symmetric matrix), while intensity-modulated stepping increases decay at annotated phase transitions. Both mechanisms preserve SSD's N-semiseparable structure and O(d) per-frame cost.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 19, 04:00

**Background**: Online surgical phase recognition requires predicting the current phase from past frames alone, with per-frame cost independent of elapsed length. Structured state-space duality (SSD) models, such as Mamba2, offer efficient sequence modeling but suffer from state overwriting and decay issues when applied to long, visually similar surgical videos. State regramming and intensity-modulated stepping are novel mechanisms designed to overcome these challenges.

## [Bounded-Differences Inequality for Non-Isotropic Gaussians with AI-Assisted Proof](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.27563) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper proves a new bounded-differences inequality for functions of non-isotropic Gaussian vectors, where the concentration bound depends on the condition number of the covariance matrix. It applies this result to answer a question by Simone Bombari about the subgaussianity of sign-quantized linear maps Y = sgn(Wx). This work provides a new tool for analyzing concentration properties of nonlinear transformations of correlated Gaussian vectors, which is relevant in high-dimensional statistics and signal processing. The AI-assisted discovery narrative also highlights the evolving role of AI in mathematical research and the importance of proper attribution. The inequality is elementary and applies to any bounded function with bounded coordinate differences, with the bound depending on the condition number κ(Σ). The authors note that an argument for the sign function case was initially suggested by Gemini 3.5 Flash without attribution, but it closely resembles Lemma 4.5 of Barber and Kolar (2018), and this revision corrects the attribution.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 19, 04:00

**Background**: McDiarmid's inequality is a classical concentration inequality for functions of independent random variables, but it does not directly apply to correlated variables like non-isotropic Gaussians. The condition number of a covariance matrix measures how ill-conditioned the distribution is, affecting concentration. Sign quantization is a common operation in signal processing and neural networks, and understanding the subgaussianity of such maps is important for theoretical guarantees.

## [BRo-JEPA: Learning Modular Arithmetic via Block Rotations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.01372) ⭐️ 7.0/10

BRo-JEPA introduces a block-rotation predictor in a JEPA-style world model, representing modular arithmetic operations as rotations in latent space. This enables strict zero-shot generalization to unseen operations, achieving 99.44% accuracy on MNIST and 94.35% on EMNIST, compared to 54.54% and 25.13% for the best supervised baseline. This work demonstrates that world models can learn algebraic rules when latent transformations encode the underlying modular structure, offering a promising direction for improving AI reasoning and generalization. It could influence future research in representation learning and world models, particularly for tasks requiring systematic generalization. The model is trained only on primitive operations ±1, yet generalizes to all modular operations. The block-rotation predictor applies actions as rotations, aligning latent representations with the cyclic structure of modular arithmetic. The code is available on GitHub.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 19, 04:00

**Background**: JEPA (Joint Embedding Predictive Architecture) is a self-supervised learning framework proposed by Yann LeCun for building world models that predict future states from sensory inputs. Modular arithmetic is a system where numbers wrap around after reaching a modulus, exhibiting a cyclic structure. Traditional supervised and JEPA baselines with operation embeddings fail to extrapolate to unseen operations, whereas BRo-JEPA leverages rotations to capture this structure.

<a id="item-261"></a>
## [Selective Importance Sampling Reduces Variance in Off-Policy RL for LLM Alignment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04728) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces Selective Importance Sampling (SIS), a plug-in method that uses token-level rejection sampling to treat accepted tokens as on-policy, assigning them unit importance scores while retaining standard importance sampling correction for rejected tokens. This approach is theoretically shown to reduce the gap between token-level and sequence-level off-policy gradient estimators. This work addresses a critical challenge in RL post-training for LLMs: the variance explosion caused by compounding token-level importance ratios in off-policy settings. By providing a theoretically grounded and computationally lightweight solution, SIS can improve the stability and robustness of RLHF and other alignment algorithms, benefiting the broader LLM alignment community. SIS is a plug-in that only modifies the importance ratio in the policy loss, adding negligible wall-clock overhead, and can be combined with various RL post-training algorithms. Experiments on dense and MoE LLMs across math and agent benchmarks show consistent improvements in all objectives and substantially stronger robustness under off-policy data.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 19, 04:00

**Background**: Reinforcement learning (RL) post-training for large language models (LLMs) often follows a 'rollout then update' paradigm, which inevitably produces off-policy training data. Importance sampling (IS) is commonly used to correct for this distribution mismatch, but token-level importance ratios compound over long sequences, leading to severe variance. Rejection sampling is a related technique that generates exact samples from a target distribution by accepting or rejecting proposals, which inspired the SIS approach.
