WAM-Diff2 introduces a three-stage hierarchical distillation framework that converts pre-trained autoregressive VLA models into parallel discrete diffusion models, achieving a 2.8x decoding speedup and up to 15.1x acceleration with system-level optimizations. This work addresses the critical latency and exposure bias issues in autoregressive VLA models, enabling efficient deployment for real-time autonomous driving. It bridges the gap between cognitive reasoning and execution efficiency, potentially accelerating the adoption of VLA models in production systems. The framework uses progressive block-wise adaptation, block-wise distillation, and model-wise cross-scale distillation to preserve semantic foundations while accelerating inference. Evaluations across driving understanding, perception, and planning benchmarks show performance parity with autoregressive baselines, with speedups achieved via FlashInfer and CUDA Graphs.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 19, 04:00

**Background**: Vision-Language-Action (VLA) models combine visual and linguistic reasoning to generate driving actions. Autoregressive models generate tokens sequentially, causing high latency and exposure bias, while diffusion models generate actions in parallel but often lack holistic reasoning when trained from scratch. WAM-Diff2 aims to combine the strengths of both by distilling autoregressive knowledge into a diffusion model.

## [Guideline-as-Oracle: Zero-Annotation Training Boosts Triage Agent Accuracy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04772) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces Guideline-as-Oracle (GAO), a zero-annotation training approach that compiles American Academy of Ophthalmology guidance into a 70-row operational rule table to supervise 3,000 training dialogues. Fine-tuning a 9B backbone yields GAO-Triage, improving agreement with an operational reference from 61.7% to 74.1% and emergent-case recall from 9.5% to 69.0%. This work addresses the high cost and privacy restrictions of expert annotation in medical AI, offering a scalable zero-annotation alternative. It demonstrates significant performance gains over general-purpose systems without requiring frontier models at inference, which could accelerate deployment of medical triage agents in privacy-sensitive settings. The authors catalog eight construction strategies for converting rules into dialogues, including cited-row tier assignment and label repair, and characterize their evidential status. Permuting label-dialogue assignments collapses the model to a constant-routine predictor, indicating the signal lies in guideline-derived assignment, and label repair coincides with the disappearance of a late-training safety degradation.

This paper introduces a three-stage framework for generating visually aligned follow-up edit suggestions in image-creation conversational systems, using real user data from Qwen App and reinforcement learning to align suggestions with user preferences. The framework reduces visual inconsistency from 3.7% to 0.9% in a live A/B test and improves recommendation CTR by 32.70%. This work addresses a novel and practical problem in multimodal conversational AI, highlighting the importance of multimodal recommendations in image-creation tasks. The significant improvements in user engagement metrics demonstrate the potential for enhancing user experience in real-world applications. The framework consists of three stages: building a human-reviewed table of follow-up editing intents and fine-tuning a multimodal policy via SFT; optimizing the policy with multi-objective reinforcement learning using user click feedback; and introducing a visual verifier to reduce visual inconsistencies. The study collected 100,000 real multi-turn image-creation conversation samples from Qwen App, finding that 80.1% are image-dependent.

## [FUSE: Frame-Unified Stress Estimation from Facial Video](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10442) ⭐️ 7.0/10

FUSE introduces a novel framework that processes entire facial video recordings as a single 2D representation, eliminating the need for temporal windowing. It achieves a highest test accuracy of 69.44% at a temporal stride of t=15, with the full-frame configuration reaching 69.03%. This approach simplifies stress detection from facial video by removing the need for window length, overlap, and aggregation choices, potentially improving temporal modeling. It could impact affective computing and non-intrusive mental health monitoring by enabling more efficient and accurate analysis of long recordings. FUSE folds the temporal dimension into the channel dimension, creating a high-dimensional input processed by a unified asymmetric-attention architecture. At a temporal stride of t=1, it retains a full 120-second recording as one input (3,600 frames at 30 fps), with computational cost ranging from 12.48 to 348.78 GFLOPs across stride configurations.

**Background**: Traditional video-based stress detection methods typically divide recordings into short temporal windows before classification, requiring choices about window length, overlap, and aggregation. The method is evaluated on a 58-subject stress dataset using a stratified subject-level protocol.

## [12-CNOT Double Qubit Excitation Gate Achieved](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11733) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers have reported the first 12-CNOT decomposition of the double qubit excitation operator, reducing the CNOT count from the previous state-of-the-art 13-CNOT circuits. The new circuit also achieves a 27% reduction in CNOT depth and a 25% reduction in total circuit depth. This optimization is significant because the double qubit excitation operator is used hundreds or thousands of times in practical quantum algorithms, so reducing its CNOT count and depth can lead to substantial overall resource savings. Fewer CNOT gates typically result in more reliable quantum computations with reduced error rates. The new circuit adds only two extra one-qubit gates compared to the lowest one-qubit gate count (11) among previous SOTA circuits. The authors compared their circuit with previous SOTA circuits across four metrics: CNOT count, CNOT depth, total circuit depth, and one-qubit gate count.

**Background**: Quantum circuits are composed of elementary gates, and CNOT gates are a key two-qubit gate that can introduce errors. Optimizing the CNOT count and depth is crucial for improving the efficiency and reliability of quantum algorithms. The double qubit excitation operator is a fundamental building block in quantum chemistry and other applications, making its efficient implementation highly valuable.

## [AI Model Internally Distinguishes Falsehood from Impossibility](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12852) ⭐️ 7.0/10

An activation study on Gemma 3 4B IT reveals that while the model's outputs conflate contingent falsehood with contradiction, its internal representations can separate impossible from true statements (AUC 0.93) and even distinguish necessary from contingent falsehoods (AUC 1.00). This finding suggests that language models may encode subtle philosophical distinctions internally, which could improve interpretability and reliability of AI reasoning. It also provides empirical evidence for a long-standing philosophical distinction, potentially bridging AI research and philosophy of language. The study used 85 prompts from 17 philosophical families and a topic-matched modality set, with a linear truth probe achieving AUC 0.93 for impossible vs. true, but only 0.20 for impossible vs. false. An impossibility probe separated necessary from contingent falsehoods at AUC 1.00, peaking at layer 15 with balanced accuracy 0.97 (Bonferroni-adjusted P=0.018).

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 19, 04:00

**Background**: Truth probes are linear classifiers trained on language model activations to read out truth-related structures. The necessary/contingent distinction is a philosophical concept where necessary falsehoods (e.g., contradictions) are false in all possible worlds, while contingent falsehoods are false in the actual world but could be true in others. This study explores whether a small open-weight model (Gemma 3 4B IT) internally represents this distinction.

## [NICE: Scale-Stable Noise Corruption for GNN Explanations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.16038) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper identifies a scale drift problem in element-wise masking for GNN explainers and proposes a scale-stable noise corruption perturbation method, leading to the NICE framework and the Boundary-Integrated Gradient (BIG) for edge attributions. This work addresses a fundamental flaw in perturbation-based GNN explainers, improving the reliability and faithfulness of explanations, which is critical for deploying GNNs in high-stakes domains like drug discovery and social network analysis. The proposed Noise Corruption (NC) perturbs each message with matched-norm random-direction corruption while preserving the expected squared message norm, and NICE learns a Stochastic Restoration Boundary (SRB) to balance restoration and compactness. Experiments show improved explanation performance and faithfulness while reducing scale drift.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 19, 04:00

**Background**: Post-hoc GNN explainers often use a Perturb-Query paradigm, where importance is inferred by querying model predictions on perturbed inputs. Element-wise masking (EM) is a common perturbation method, but it can cause scale drift, where edge-induced messages are suppressed toward zero, accumulating across message-passing layers and conflating information corruption with scale changes. This paper proposes Noise Corruption (NC) as a scale-stable alternative to EM.

## [Memory Is Communication: The Remembering-Signaling Frontier](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.17053) ⭐️ 6.0/10

This paper introduces a theoretical framework for bounded agents to allocate resources between memory and communication, defining an achievable region and its efficient boundary, the remembering-signaling frontier. Preliminary referential game experiments show that target repetition shortens successful messages, while predictability from a hidden cyclic rule does not. This work bridges information theory, bounded rationality, and emergent communication, offering a principled way to study how agents balance internal memory and external communication. It could inform the design of more efficient multi-agent systems and deepen our understanding of human and artificial communication. The framework assumes fixed task and decision rule, and defines an achievable region for memory and message rate pairs under resource limits. The preliminary experiments use referential games, where target repetition coincided with shorter messages, but hidden cyclic rule predictability did not, suggesting nuanced effects of history on communication.

The achievable rate region is a core concept in network information theory, defining feasible rate combinations for reliable communication.

This paper introduces 'cooperative observation' as a framework for personal AI, where the system's observation of the user is shaped by a feedback loop of usefulness, trust, and consent. A preliminary single-subject account from the Organizm prototype, used over six months, is reported. This framework addresses the observation bottleneck in personal AI, potentially improving how AI systems build user models while respecting user control. It could influence the design of future personal AI assistants, making them more trustworthy and effective. The paper argues that broader observation does not automatically improve assistance; instead, a bounded system must select and compress information for the task. The feedback loop among usefulness, trust, and future access is central to the proposed framework, with evaluation directions outlined for measuring observation quality.

The best model achieved 39.12% accuracy, a threefold improvement over the untrained base model's 12.37%. This work explores the application of RL fine-tuning to a niche but important domain—signal processing—using small 3B models, which are more accessible and cost-effective than larger models. ## [HLSR: Selective Hybrid Live-Forecast Rerouting to Ease Urban Congestion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.18056) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper introduces HLSR, a selective hybrid live-forecast vehicle rerouting framework that fuses real-time and predicted traffic data to reduce congestion with limited intervention scope. It builds on dual-threshold congestion detection, calibrated upstream selection, and driver-tailored travel-time prediction, adding approaching-vehicle expansion, travel-time-weighted k-shortest-path generation, and a horizon-dependent hybrid speed model. This work addresses the practical limitation of network-wide rerouting that requires replanning nearly all vehicles, which is often infeasible in real deployments. By selectively rerouting only a subset of vehicles, HLSR could make dynamic congestion avoidance more scalable and applicable in real-world traffic management systems, potentially reducing travel time and emissions. HLSR introduces approaching-vehicle expansion to identify vehicles likely to enter congested areas, travel-time-weighted k-shortest-path generation to provide alternative routes, and a horizon-dependent hybrid live-forecast segment speed for multi-cost route allocation. The approach aims to balance effectiveness with limited intervention scope, though specific simulation results or performance metrics are not detailed in the abstract.

**Background**: Urban traffic congestion is a major problem that reduces productivity and increases travel costs and emissions. Traditional network-wide rerouting approaches assume that nearly all vehicles can be replanned every decision period, which is impractical in real-world settings. HLSR combines live traffic data with short-horizon forecasts and selectively reroutes only a subset of vehicles, making the approach more feasible for real-time congestion avoidance.

## [Landmark Methods Outperform Random Walk for Graph Distance Estimation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.16916) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new study compares random walk sampling with landmark-based methods (SEF and Eppstein-Wang) for estimating average distances in large static graphs, finding that landmark-based approaches are more efficient and accurate. The Eppstein-Wang algorithm achieved error margins as low as 0.02%. This research provides practical guidance for graph analytics on large-scale networks, where computing exact average distances is computationally prohibitive. The findings help practitioners choose efficient approximation methods, potentially improving performance in social network analysis, web graph mining, and other applications. The study tested on static, undirected, unweighted graphs (both unipartite and bipartite). Random walk required at least 15% of nodes for accuracy, while landmark methods used probabilistic data structures like HyperLogLog for memory efficiency. The Eppstein-Wang algorithm showed higher reliability on unipartite graphs than bipartite graphs.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 19, 04:00

**Background**: Average distance is a key metric in graph analytics, but computing it exactly for large graphs is computationally intensive and memory-limited. Random walk sampling is a common approximation technique, while landmark-based methods select a subset of nodes (landmarks) and use their distances to estimate the average. The Size Estimation Framework (SEF) by Cohen and the Eppstein-Wang (EW) algorithm are two landmark-based approaches; SEF uses random ranking and least-element lists, while EW uses a logarithmic number of breadth-first searches. HyperLogLog is a probabilistic data structure for estimating the number of unique elements, used here for memory-efficient neighbor exploration.

## [From Abductive Explanations to Global Logical Rules for Node Classification in SGCs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.17103) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper proposes a logic-based framework that uses minimal abductive explanations as an intermediate representation to extract global logical rules for node classification in Simple Graph Convolution (SGC) networks. This approach addresses the limitation of existing methods like LogicXGNN, which rely on explanatory subgraphs that may contain redundant, node-specific structural information. This work advances explainable AI for graph neural networks by providing a more principled way to derive compact, generalizable logical rules, which can improve trust and interpretability in applications like social network analysis or bioinformatics. It also highlights the potential of abductive reasoning as a tool for model-agnostic explanation, potentially inspiring further research in this direction. The framework computes, for each node, a minimal set of node-feature pairs sufficient to preserve the predicted class, then trains decision trees on these explanations to extract global logical rules. Experiments on benchmark datasets demonstrate that the proposed framework produces compact global rules while maintaining high fidelity to the original SGC model.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 19, 04:00

**Background**: Graph Neural Networks (GNNs) are powerful models for node classification but are often seen as black boxes. Logic-based explanation methods, such as LogicXGNN, aim to extract global logical rules from explanatory subgraphs, but these subgraphs may contain redundant, node-specific information. Abductive explanations, which identify minimal sets of features sufficient for a prediction, offer a more principled intermediate representation. Simple Graph Convolution (SGC) is a simplified variant of GCNs that removes nonlinearities and collapses weight matrices between layers, making it more efficient while retaining competitive performance.

<a id="item-287"></a>
## [MoFE: FNO-MoE Hybrid for Cryptocurrency Forecasting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.17342) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper introduces MoFE, a novel deep learning framework that integrates Fourier Neural Operators (FNOs) into a Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) architecture for cryptocurrency price forecasting. Experiments on Bitcoin data from January 2020 to December 2025 show state-of-the-art performance in T+1 and T+5 horizons, with improved directional accuracy and information coefficient. This work addresses the persistent phase-lag problem in financial time series forecasting, which is a significant challenge for deep learning models. By combining FNO's ability to learn continuous function mappings with MoE's adaptive strategy switching, MoFE could improve trading strategies and risk management in cryptocurrency markets. MoFE uses specialized adaptive FNO (AFNO) and Convolution dual-domain experts to capture global spectral trends, cyclical adjustments, and microstructures, while a dynamic gating mechanism enables adaptive strategy switching across market regimes. The model is grounded in stochastic differential equations, conceptualizing volatility as a superposition of multi-frequency components, and demonstrates high Sharpe ratios in simulated trading.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 19, 04:00

**Background**: Fourier Neural Operators (FNOs) generalize neural networks to learn mappings between continuous functions, making them suitable for capturing global spectral trends in time series. Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) is a machine learning technique where multiple expert networks divide a problem space into homogeneous regions, enabling adaptive strategy selection. Cryptocurrency forecasting is challenging due to non-stationarity, regime shifts, and multi-scale stochastic dependencies, which conventional deep learning models often fail to capture.

## [Student-AI Question Types in CS2: A Few-Shot Taxonomy Analysis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.17919) ⭐️ 6.0/10

This study applies the Graesser et al. taxonomy to classify 830 student-AI interactions in two CS2 programming tasks, using a few-shot learning approach to automatically categorize questions into 18 types. The findings reveal that a small subset of question types accounts for the majority of inquiries, and the distribution shifts as tasks progress. This research provides empirical insights into how students use AI for learning in programming contexts, which can inform the design of AI-assisted educational tools and pedagogical strategies. Understanding dominant question types helps educators anticipate student needs and tailor support, potentially improving AI-based tutoring systems. The study uses a few-shot learning approach for automatic classification, which is efficient when labeled data is scarce. The analysis covers 830 interactions from CS2 students across two programming tasks, and the taxonomy includes 18 distinct question types.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 19, 04:00

**Background**: Question asking is integral to learning, yet students often ask few questions in classrooms. With the rise of generative AI, students increasingly interact with AI systems for problem solving. The Graesser et al. taxonomy provides a framework for classifying questions into types, and few-shot learning enables automatic classification with limited examples, making it feasible to analyze large-scale student-AI interactions.

## [ChatPlanner: LLM Framework for Personalized Transit Routing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.15315) ⭐️ 6.0/10

ChatPlanner is a novel framework that uses fine-tuned LLMs with Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) to extract user preferences from natural language and integrate them into public transit routing algorithms. The paper presents four experiments demonstrating its feasibility, accuracy, and computational tractability. This work bridges natural language understanding and transportation optimization, enabling more personalized and user-friendly transit routing. It could improve user satisfaction and adoption of public transit by offering route alternatives that align with individual preferences, which existing planners often overlook. The framework uses eight personas and five contexts to create preference-aware datasets for fine-tuning and RAG. The combination of fine-tuning and RAG achieves the highest accuracy in routing information extraction and rubric-consistent user preference interpretation, while latency evaluation confirms computational tractability.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 19, 04:00

**Background**: Public transit routing algorithms traditionally optimize for generic criteria like travel time or number of transfers, but struggle to incorporate individual user preferences. Large Language Models (LLMs) can understand natural language, and Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) allows them to access external data to improve responses without retraining. ChatPlanner combines these technologies to extract preferences from conversational queries and adjust the routing objective function accordingly.

## [Hybrid Genetic Algorithm Cuts Port Container Handling Time by 15-20%](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.08534) ⭐️ 6.0/10

This paper introduces QCDC-DR-GA, a hybrid genetic algorithm that jointly optimizes quay crane dual-cycling and dockyard rehandle minimization, achieving 15-20% reduction in total operation time for large ships compared to existing methods. This matters because port operations are a critical bottleneck in global trade, and the reported 15-20% time savings can significantly reduce vessel turnaround times and operational costs without requiring infrastructure investments. It also highlights the importance of integrated optimization over isolated approaches in complex logistics systems. The algorithm uses specialized crossover and mutation strategies, and statistical validation via two-tailed paired t-tests confirms significant improvements at the 5% significance level. The paper addresses the NP-hard problem of integrating Quay Crane Dual-Cycling (QCDC) with dockyard rehandle minimization, showing that isolated optimization is inefficient.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 19, 04:00

**Background**: Quay crane dual-cycling is a technique that overlaps unloading and loading tasks at the quayside to improve crane productivity, while dockyard rehandles are extra container movements that increase labor, fuel, and equipment wear. The paper proposes a hybrid genetic algorithm to optimize both aspects simultaneously, as they are interdependent. Genetic algorithms are metaheuristics inspired by natural selection, commonly used for NP-hard optimization problems.

## [MSAM Improves Hyperspectral Segmentation for Autonomous Driving](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.18682) ⭐️ 6.0/10

This paper introduces a Multi-Scale Spectral Attention Module (MSAM) integrated into UNet's skip connections for hyperspectral semantic segmentation in autonomous driving. It achieves average improvements of 2.32% in mIoU and 2.88% in mF1 over the baseline UNet-SC. This work addresses the challenge of efficiently processing high-dimensional hyperspectral data for enhanced environmental perception in autonomous driving, especially under challenging conditions. The proposed module offers a computationally efficient way to improve segmentation accuracy, which could benefit the broader computer vision and AD communities. MSAM uses three parallel 1D convolutions with kernel sizes ranging from 1 to 11, combined with adaptive feature aggregation. Ablation studies show that optimal kernel combinations are dataset-specific, with configurations like (1;5;11) and (3;7;11) performing particularly well, while maintaining competitive GPU performance.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 19, 04:00

**Background**: Hyperspectral imaging (HSI) captures information across many narrow spectral bands, providing rich data for environmental perception in autonomous driving. Semantic segmentation classifies each pixel into predefined categories, and UNet is a popular architecture for this task, using skip connections to preserve spatial details. Attention mechanisms help models focus on relevant features, and multi-scale approaches capture details at different resolutions.

## [2.5D U-Net Enables Real-Time Gaseous Microemboli Detection in TEE](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.22258) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A feasibility study demonstrates that a 2.5D U-Net architecture can detect gaseous microemboli (GME) in real-time transesophageal echocardiography (TEE) recordings, outperforming classical spot detection algorithms and 2D U-Net while maintaining real-time execution speed. This work addresses a critical need in cardiac surgery and interventional procedures where GME detection is challenging due to operator-dependent views and high velocity. Improved real-time detection could enhance patient safety by enabling timely intervention to prevent neurological complications. The model was trained and tested on a pilot dataset of eight TEE recordings (60 fps, 600x800 pixels) from eight patients, achieving a precision of 92.55% and recall of 80.54% under a three-pixel radius-tolerant grace-zone evaluation. The average inference time was 0.12 seconds per batch, and external validation on GME-negative data showed low spurious detection rates.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 19, 04:00

**Background**: Gaseous microemboli are common complications during cardiac structural interventions and can lead to neurological damage. Transesophageal echocardiography (TEE) is a convenient monitoring tool, but manual detection is difficult due to high velocity and similar background structures. The 2.5D U-Net is a variant of the U-Net architecture that processes volumetric data without full 3D convolutions, reducing memory requirements while retaining spatial context. This study explores its application to real-time GME segmentation.

## [UltraArUco: 6x Faster Marker Tracking for Mobile AR](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13584) ⭐️ 6.0/10

UltraArUco introduces a lightweight multilingual library that reduces per-frame latency in marker-based mobile AR tracking by six times compared to OpenCV-based implementations. The framework is validated through an interactive piano simulation using static ArUco markers on keys and hand-mounted markers for gesture recognition. This significant latency reduction makes marker-based AR more responsive and practical for resource-constrained mobile devices, potentially expanding AR applications in education, entertainment, and interactive installations. It demonstrates that optimized wrappers can achieve substantial performance gains without specialized hardware, lowering the barrier for AR development. UltraArUco uses a distributed Wi-Fi architecture to connect a mobile device (camera input) with a PC-based visual application, enabling responsive interactions. The system maintains high accuracy while achieving the six-fold latency reduction, and its low system requirements make it suitable for resource-constrained mobile AR applications.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 19, 04:00

**Background**: ArUco markers are synthetic square markers with a black border and an inner binary matrix that encodes an ID, commonly used in augmented reality and robotics for pose estimation. Marker-based tracking uses a camera to detect these markers and determine their position and orientation, enabling virtual objects to be overlaid in real time. Traditional OpenCV-based implementations often suffer from high latency on mobile devices, limiting interactivity.
